# Studiior

Multi-tenant SaaS for boutique fitness studios (Pilates, yoga, barre). Supabase + Next.js. Six-month V1, public launch 2 March 2027, ten design partners first.

Tenant one is Reform Collective, the founder's own studio. **Its data is production data.** Treat member PII accordingly from day one.

---

## Read before writing code

| File | What it governs |
|---|---|
| `docs/STUDIIOR_PRODUCT_BIBLE.md` | The source of truth. Vision, UX and per-chapter MVP scope. Above everything below. |
| `docs/STUDIIOR_V1_DECISIONS.md` | Settled decisions. Canonical. Code that contradicts it is wrong. |
| `docs/STUDIIOR_V1_DATA_MODEL.md` | Every table, column, index, RLS approach, concurrency rules |
| `docs/STUDIIOR_V1_BUSINESS_RULES.md` | Booking, cancellation, waitlist, credits, memberships, challenges, AI |
| `docs/STUDIIOR_V1_PERMISSIONS.md` | Five roles against every action. Becomes RLS policies directly. |
| `docs/SETTINGS_WITHOUT_UI.md` | Which settings columns a studio cannot reach. Re-run `scripts/audit-settings-ui.py` after any migration that adds one. |

`docs/STUDIIOR_PRODUCT_BIBLE.md` governs scope above all of these. Read it before arguing about what is in V1.

**Its chapter numbers are not what this file used to claim.** There is no "Chapter 8's seven modules" and no "Chapter 7 exclusion list": Ch. 7 is the Booking Engine, Ch. 8 is Memberships & Billing, and Ch. 9 and Ch. 20 do not exist at all. Scope lives in a per-chapter **MVP Scope** section — Ch. 4 (dashboard), Ch. 5 (calendar), 6.23 (CRM), Ch. 7 (booking), 8.18 (billing), Ch. 10 (community), Ch. 12 (analytics) — each splitting Phase 1 / Phase 2 / Phase 3. Volumes 1–13 at the top are an outline of what the Bible will contain, not content; only chapters 1, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 10, 12, 16 and 19 are written.

The seven modules — Scheduling & Booking · Member CRM · Memberships & Payments · Your Member App · AI Morning Brief · Challenges · Reports — are **this project's narrowing** of the Bible, not a quotation from it. They are a defensible six-month cut. Where they and the Bible disagree, say so out loud rather than picking silently. **Known disagreement:** Ch. 10 puts a community feed, reactions, announcements and friend connections in launch scope; this project excludes them (`docs/STUDIIOR_V1_DECISIONS.md`, "Excluded from V1"). Unresolved.

## Current state

One hundred and seventy-four migrations, applying clean from `supabase db reset`:

- **Decision 41 — per-studio sign-up confirmation redirect (NO MIGRATION; app + Supabase config only).** `app/member/actions.ts` `signUp` called `supabase.auth.signUp` with no `emailRedirectTo`, so the confirmation link used the project-wide **Site URL** — one value for every studio — and members confirming at `reformcollective.studiior.app` landed on the **staff login** at `app.studiior.com`. Now `signUp` (and a new `requestPasswordReset`) pass `emailRedirectTo = ${memberOrigin}/auth/callback?next=${next}`, where `memberOrigin` is **`currentMemberOrigin()`** (new in `lib/tenant.ts` — the ACTUAL request `Host`: `https://{slug}.studiior.app` hosted, `http://{slug}.lvh.me:3000`/`.localhost` locally, never a hardcoded slug, never Site URL) and `next` is the signup/login page's own `?next` (the embed passes `/class/{id}`), default `/`. The builder **`buildAuthCallback`** and the open-redirect guard **`safeNext`** (a RELATIVE path only — absolute URL, `//host`, `/\`, control char or non-string → `/`) live in **`lib/auth-redirect.mjs`** (plain ESM so `node --test` runs them with no bundler — this project has no JS runner and Node 20 here cannot import a `.ts`; a sibling `.d.ts` types the app under `moduleResolution: bundler`). New route **`app/member/auth/callback/route.ts`** handles `?code=` (`exchangeCodeForSession`) and `?token_hash=&type=` (`verifyOtp`), sets the session via the `@supabase/ssr` cookie client (a Route Handler can write cookies), then redirects to a `safeNext` `next` or, on failure, the studio's own member `/login?error=confirm`. **The redirect base is the request `Host` header, NOT `url.origin`** — behind the middleware rewrite and bound to `localhost` in dev, `url.origin` is the server origin, and redirecting there is the staff-login bug itself. Middleware lets member `/auth/callback` through **without a session** (rewrite only, skip the refresh — the route owns the cookie write). `signup`/`login` pages carry `?next=` through. **No new anon surface (still eleven), no SQL, no migration; drift IN STEP unchanged.** Tests: `test/auth_redirect.test.mjs` (`node --test test/auth_redirect.test.mjs`, 4 assertions — member host → correct callback origin; `next` must be relative; absolute/`//`/backslash rejected) + the callback live-tested (bad `code` and bad `token_hash` each → the member host's `/login?error=confirm`; absolute `next` neutralised). Full chain proven locally through **real GoTrue**: the confirmation email's `verify?...redirect_to=` carried `http://reform.lvh.me:3000/auth/callback?next=%2Fclass%2Fabc123` (not Site URL), which also proves the local `additional_redirect_urls` allowlist is load-bearing — an un-allowlisted host makes GoTrue silently fall back to Site URL. **HOSTED DASHBOARD — Deanna must set (once):** (1) Authentication → URL Configuration → **Redirect URLs**: add **`https://*.studiior.app/**`**; (2) **Site URL**: no longer relied on for member confirmation — leave it at a neutral page (it must not point at the member or staff login); (3) Authentication → Email Templates → **Confirm signup**: paste `supabase/templates/confirm.html` (project-wide, studio-agnostic — per-studio branding of auth emails is a later decision via the Send Email auth hook); (4) confirm the auth email **sender is `Studiior <accounts@studiior.app>`** (SMTP settings; local shows the CLI default `Admin`). `supabase/config.toml` already carries the local member globs and the confirm template for `supabase start`.

- **174** Decision 33 amendment — the emailed calendar was broken in three ways; the in-app download routes and the webcal feed were NEVER affected (they return the `.ics` as the raw response body — no base64, no Resend — so only the emailed attachment and the human date changed). Diagnosed read-only against hosted first (Deanna's REFORMER FLOW confirmation: a 542-byte valid `.ics` delivered as a **0-byte** `studiior.ics` with no Gmail card). **(1) THE EMPTY ATTACHMENT WAS POSTGRES MIME-WRAPPING THE BASE64.** `send_via_resend` built the attachment `content` with `encode(convert_to(p_ics,'UTF8'),'base64')`, and Postgres's base64 encoder inserts a newline every 76 chars (RFC 2045) — the rendered base64 carried 9 embedded newlines and Resend delivered 0 bytes. Fix: `translate(encode(…,'base64'), E'\n', '')` — single-line, what every Resend SDK sends. `create or replace`, signature unchanged, ACL preserved + asserted in the migration. **(2) NO GMAIL CARD BECAUSE THE CALENDAR WAS METHOD:PUBLISH WITH NO ORGANIZER/ATTENDEE.** Gmail only renders its event/RSVP card for an **invitation** — METHOD:REQUEST with an ORGANIZER and an ATTENDEE matching the recipient (Apple Mail is lenient and cards a valid PUBLISH attachment, so fix 1 alone restored Apple Mail). So the **emailed** calendars become invitations: `booking_confirmed`/`class_moved`/`class_cancelled` (member) and `instructor_assigned`/`booking_for_instructor` (instructor) carry **ORGANIZER = the studio's contact email** (fallback `notifications@{from_domain}`) and **ATTENDEE = the recipient's own email** (`RSVP=TRUE;PARTSTAT=NEEDS-ACTION`), METHOD **REQUEST** (add/update) or **CANCEL** (`class_cancelled`, `STATUS:CANCELLED`, same UID, higher SEQUENCE); `class_moved` keeps the same UID at a higher SEQUENCE. Mechanism: `ics_vevent` gains optional `p_organizer`/`p_attendee` (raw `ORGANIZER:mailto:` / `ATTENDEE;…:mailto:` lines, FOLDED not escaped — RFC params use `;`/`,` which `ics_escape` would corrupt), a **drop+recreate** 9→11 args (defaults null; the 9-arg positional calls still resolve); `ics_member_vevent`/`ics_instructor_vevent` gain optional attendee/organizer emails that DEFAULT NULL, so the unchanged route/feed callers stay PUBLISH-shaped with no ATTENDEE (drop+recreate, ACL re-asserted service-role only); `notification_ics` resolves the recipient email (member row for member templates, `studio_staff` row for instructor templates) + the studio organizer and sets REQUEST/CANCEL (`create or replace`); `send_via_resend` derives `content_type; method=` from the calendar's own METHOD line via `substring(p_ics from 'METHOD:([A-Z]+)')` for REQUEST as well as CANCEL/PUBLISH. **The download routes (`member_class_ics`/`instructor_class_ics`/`instructor_month_ics`) and `calendar_feed` still wrap `ics_calendar('PUBLISH', …)` with no attendee — a file the person adds, not an invitation.** Trade-off, accepted: the emailed `.ics` now carries the recipient's email as ATTENDEE and reads as a studio invitation (RSVP replies route to ORGANIZER) — the only shape Gmail cards. **(3) THE DATE CARRIED NO YEAR** — `{when}`/`{old_when}`/`{grace_ends}` were `'FMDay FMDD FMMonth[, HH24:MI]'` ("Monday 9 November, 07:00"). The named families get `YYYY`, re-issued from their EXACT current definitions (`pg_get_functiondef` + a single literal replace, so no transcription drift — which also caught that `queue_booking_notifications`'s current body carries the 163 free-first lines the 660000 file lacked): the booking emails (`queue_booking_notifications`, `queue_class_moved`, `queue_occurrence_cancelled`), the cover/substitution family (`request_cover`, `approve_cover_request`, `decline_cover_request`, `accept_cover`, `sweep_cover_escalations`, `queue_substitution`, `reassign_occurrence`), the shift/assignment family (`apply_for_shift`, `approve_shift_application`, `decline_shift_application`, `open_shift`, `queue_instructor_assigned`) and `queue_platform_warning`. `class_reminder` ("tomorrow") and the weekday-only brief text are left alone; `create_occurrence`/`move_occurrence` already carried the year (their availability warning). **Found year-less and DEFERRED (not the named families, flagged for a follow-up decision):** `queue_waitlist_offer`, `queue_waitlist_missed`, `queue_instructor_booking_alert`, `sweep_guest_waivers`, `sweep_instructor_confirmations`, and the staff previews `archive_impact`/`availability_conflicts`. `test/ics_test.sql` (UUID `ca1e`, now 23): the confirm is METHOD:REQUEST + ORGANIZER + member ATTENDEE + a four-digit year in the payload `when`; the move is REQUEST at a higher SEQUENCE; the cancel is CANCEL at a higher SEQUENCE than the confirm for the same UID with the same ORGANIZER/ATTENDEE; the download route stays PUBLISH with no ATTENDEE/ORGANIZER; and the attachment base64 is single-line and round-trips to the identical `.ics` bytes (with a teeth-assert that the raw base64 WAS wrapped). Decision recorded in `docs/STUDIIOR_V1_DECISIONS.md` (### Amendment under ## 33) before code. **ON HOSTED** (`20260831790000`, `remote` verified): `check-hosted-drift.sh` **IN STEP** (433 functions), anon surface **exactly ELEVEN** (unchanged — the ics builders are `service_role`/`authenticated` only, never anon), `check-null-guards.py` **IN STEP**, `audit-settings-ui.py` no new orphan (no settings column touched). - **171** Decision 36 — the member app's booking horizon is the MEMBER's, not the studio default. `book_class` rule **2.1.2** resolves the window from the member's highest-priority usable plan (recurring > trial > pack, earliest expiry, carrying a window OR a daily cap), coalesced to `studio_settings.booking_window_days`. But `member_bootstrap` (`20260831680000`) exposed only `coalesce(st.booking_window_days, 30)` — studio only — and `lib/member.ts` / `app/member/book/page.tsx` built "Opens for booking …" from it, so a member on a **60-day plan** at a studio whose row still held the onboarding default **30** was told a 45-day class "opens" weeks out when book_class would have **booked** it. **The fix makes divergence impossible rather than copying the rule into TS.** Migration `20260831760000` extracts 2.1.2 into **`member_booking_window_days(p_member_id)`** — SECURITY DEFINER, guarded `is_service_context() OR is_desk_up OR is_self` which **mirrors book_class's OWN permission exactly** (`v_trusted` ≡ `is_service_context()`, both rolsuper/rolbypassrls; proven byte-identical) so calling it inside book_class can never refuse a booking book_class would allow; it replicates book_class's **exact** row selection (the same `booking_window_days IS NOT NULL OR max_bookings_per_day IS NOT NULL` filter and priority, so it picks the same plan) and coalesces to the studio value, **never a hardcoded 30**. `book_class` is re-issued (`create or replace`, ACL held) to call it for the window while keeping its own read of the same row for the daily cap — **behaviour identical, the suites prove it**. `member_bootstrap` (`create or replace`, shape unchanged so no drop) resolves `booking_window_days` through the same function. **Client:** `lib/member.ts` drops the `?? 30` fallback (`bookingWindowDays: number | null`); the book page **fails loudly in dev** on a null window and otherwise treats null as **no limit** (book_class's own reading of a null window), never inventing 30 — null only arises if a studio has no `studio_settings` row at all (the column is `int NOT NULL default 30`), a provisioning bug. **Settings:** Settings → Timetable gains **"How far ahead members can book"** (`saveBookingWindow` writing `booking_window_days`, help text *"A membership plan's own window overrides this."*), so the column leaves `audit-settings-ui.py`'s orphan list (now under HAS A WRITE PATH). `test/book_class_test.sql` (space `ffffffff`) grows a Decision-36 section: a 60-day plan lifts `member_booking_window_days` AND `member_bootstrap.booking_window_days` to 60 and book_class **books** a 45-day class (null reason); an EXPIRED plan and a no-plan member both resolve to the studio 30 and book_class refuses the 45-day class with **`outside_booking_window`** (the same reason the screen renders as "Opens for booking"); the teeth assert bootstrap **equals** what book_class resolves, and patching either side to read the studio value directly flips both to 30 (demonstrated). the desk's `record_document` paper path stays the fallback. **`self_check_in(booking, lat, lng, accuracy)`** (SECURITY DEFINER, so no member INSERT policy on `check_ins` is added): PT403 own-booking (`coalesce(v_m.user_id = auth.uid(), false)`); soft reasons not_booked/class_not_scheduled/month_not_published/window_closed/no_location/studio_has_no_location/low_accuracy/too_far; **accuracy is CAPPED** — `distance > radius + min(accuracy, cap)` → too_far, `accuracy > cap` → low_accuracy (Decision 35 change 2); PT422 waiver; writes `check_ins` method `'self'`, `checked_in_by = self`, `distance_m`/`accuracy_m`; idempotent on unique_violation → `{ok, already}`; sets `studiior.checkin_health='1'` around the insert so the AFTER-trigger health cascade (`health_on_check_in` → `refresh_member_health`, the only `members` writer on a check-in insert) may write the health columns as the member. `import_commit` re-issued with attendance method `'import'`. **`guard_member_self_update` re-issued with BOTH transaction-local flag exemptions COLUMN-NARROWED** (see the guard rule below): `studiior.waiver_signing` → `waiver_signed_at` (+`updated_at`) only; `studiior.checkin_health` → the four health columns (+`updated_at`) only — a non-allowed column under either flag raises PT403. UI: staff geofence settings (`location-panel.tsx` + `location/`, `saveLocationGeofence` writing the primary location), the member `SelfCheckIn` card (navigator.geolocation, enableHighAccuracy, sends nulls on denial → trust-based), Home hero swap when `selfCheckinAvailable`, roster method label (self·Nm / Scanned / Desk). `test/self_checkin_test.sql` (UUID `5e1c`, 20 assertions: geofence arithmetic, PT403, window_closed, no_location, too_far, low_accuracy at accuracy 4000, success+idempotent, method='self' distance stored, trust-based, waiver PT422 at self+desk) + the checkin_health teeth (a non-health write under the flag → PT403, a health write passes); `test/waiver_test.sql` gains the waiver_signing teeth (non-waiver write under the flag → PT403). Teeth-of-teeth: the WIDE guard makes each negative teeth FAIL (proven for both). **ON HOSTED** (`20260831740000`+`20260831750000`, both `remote` verified): `check-hosted-drift.sh` **IN STEP** (420 functions), advisor anon surface **exactly ELEVEN** (`self_check_in`/`earth_distance_m`/`member_waiver_current` are NOT anon — no twelfth), `check-null-guards.py` **IN STEP**, `audit-settings-ui.py` no new orphan (the geofence columns are on `locations`, written by the settings panel; studio_settings/plans unchanged). **Member probes on hosted**, as the test instructor `2202d1d7` (login `4d74ce9e`, control `role=authenticated svc=false`, every probe ended in `raise` so nothing committed): `self_check_in` on a booking the session does not own → **PT403** (own-booking guard); a direct `update members set email=…` under a held `checkin_health` flag, on a throwaway member the session owns (inserted and rolled back in the same aborted txn — nothing persisted) → **PT403** (the narrow guard blocked a non-health column). - **168** Decision 27 AMENDMENT — announcements split into two TYPES, gain a link, move down Home, and the built-in free-first banner retires in their favour. Migration 166 added a one-line dismissible strip at the top of `/book` for *pinned* posts, but "pinned" was doing two jobs (first-in-What's-on AND the strip) and a full What's-on post is the wrong shape for a one-line strip. Migration `20260831730000` adds **`announcements.kind`** (`'post'` default | `'banner'`, CHECK), **`link_url`** (https-only, CHECK `like 'https://%'`) and **`link_label`**. A **post** is what exists today (title, body, photo, dates, pinned) in the "What's on" section; a **banner** is title only (≤120 chars, CHECK `announcements_banner_len`), no body/photo, rendered as the dismissible one-line strip at the top of Home AND Book. **THE STRIP NOW SHOWS `kind='banner'` ONLY; pinned no longer drives it.** Every existing row becomes `'post'` by the default. `create_announcement`/`update_announcement` are **DROPPED and recreated** with `p_kind`/`p_link_url`/`p_link_label` (adding params is an overload not a replace — 028's trap — so the 7-arg forms are dropped and the ACL re-asserted at the bottom); they validate the kind, the ≤120 banner length, and the https link (PT422), store an empty body for a banner, and null a post's photo when it becomes a banner. `staff_announcements`/`member_announcements`/`instructor_announcements` are `create or replace` carrying `kind`/`link_url`/`link_label`. **THE BANNER STRIP IS THE ONE FILLED BAR** — `accentRamp().solid` with its measured `onSolid` text (the `--accent-solid`/`--accent-on-solid` primary-button pair), no raw hex; the X and the link arrow (`arrow-out` icon) take `onSolid` too. A same-host link opens in place, an external one in a new tab. **HOME ORDER CHANGED**: banner strips at the very top, then existing content (waiver, hero, coming up), then Challenges, then **What's on LAST, below Challenges**; Book shows banners only. Dismissal is the shared `dismiss_announcement`, so one X clears a banner on Home and Book. Multiple banners stack (studio keeps it to one or two, nothing enforced). Instructor portal shows an instructors/both banner as a strip too (no dismiss — instructors have no member row). **THE BUILT-IN FREE-FIRST `/book` BANNER IS REMOVED** — the studio's own banner is the message now; free-first eligibility is still carried by the "Book — first class free" row buttons and the class-detail block. The `dismissFreeFirst` action and the `free_first_banner` key are gone; `member_dismissals` stays for future keys. **The What's-on link button takes `--ink` on `--accent-chip` (11.42 worst), NOT `--lime-text` on the tint** — the latter measured 4.36 on Bold (accent-text-on-accent-tint, the pairing CLAUDE.md flags at 3.88), caught in the browser and fixed. `test/announcements_test.sql` grows to 29 (UUID `a11c`): a banner and a post carry `kind`; the strip is banner-only and the pinned post is NOT a strip banner; a linked banner carries its https link + a default "Learn more" label, an unlinked one carries null; a banner over 120 chars is refused PT422; an http:// link is refused PT422 and an https:// one accepted; the member_dismissals RLS holds with a generic key (the retired free_first key). **ON HOSTED** (`20260831730000`, `remote` verified): drift IN STEP (417), anon surface exactly ELEVEN (unchanged — the drop+recreate of the two writers did NOT reopen them; both `anon=false authed=true`, single signature), null-guards IN STEP, no new settings orphan (168 touched only `announcements`). **Browser-verified at 375 as an eligible lead with one banner + one post**: the banner strip is the filled bar at the top of Home and Book with a link arrow + X; What's on renders last (below Challenges) with a link button and a pinned post with no X; the free-first card is gone while the row button still reads "Book free". Contrast on all four presets + a dark navy accent — strip 4.54–14.22, What's-on link 11+ (after the fix), all ≥ 4.5.

- **167** Decision 33 AMENDMENT — the instructor per-booking channel becomes a PER-TENANT switch, no instructor opt-out. Migration 161's `instructors.email_each_booking` was the wrong shape for Reform: Reform wants its instructors to ALWAYS know when a booking lands on or leaves their class and an instructor must not be able to turn that off, so a per-instructor opt-in cannot express it. Migration `20260831720000` **drops `instructors.email_each_booking` and `set_email_each_booking`** and adds `studio_settings.instructor_booking_alerts` (boolean, NOT NULL DEFAULT **false** — off product-wide, Reform turns it on in the UI). `queue_booking_notifications` (from 163) is re-issued WITHOUT the old per-instructor email block. **COALESCED PER CLASS PER 15-MINUTE WINDOW, not per booking** — the whole point of the amendment: Reform at 21+ classes/week × 6 beds would be 100+ "one person booked" emails a week, so a busy class is at most ONE notice per quarter hour carrying the running headcount and what changed since the last notice. `queue_instructor_booking_alert(occurrence, p_gained, p_lost)` (SECURITY DEFINER, **service-role only — the grant is the boundary**) gates on instructor_id-not-null AND `instructor_booking_alerts` on AND `month_published` (Decision 25 — nothing for a draft month) AND `instructor_user_id` not null (only reaches an instructor with a login); computes `v_bucket = floor(epoch(now())/900)`, `dedupe = 'instr_booking_alert:'||occ||':'||v_bucket`, schedules at the window END (`to_timestamp((v_bucket+1)*900)`), reads the live headcount from `occurrence_seats_taken`, ACCUMULATES `n_booked`/`n_cancelled` from a still-`scheduled` prior notice for the same bucket, and upserts `on conflict (dedupe_key) do update set payload=excluded.payload where notifications.status='scheduled'` — so a second change inside the window updates the pending notice instead of sending a second email. The `change_line_text(booked,cancelled)` immutable helper renders "+N booked, M cancelled" / "+N booked" / "M cancelled" / "updated". `tg_instructor_booking_alert()` (`after insert or update of status on bookings`) fires the alert: an INSERT booked = gained, a non-booked→booked = gained, a booked→cancelled/late_cancelled = lost; demo rows skipped. The `instructor_booking_alert` template links to the roster. UI: the per-instructor toggle is GONE from `/instructor/me`; a per-tenant "Booking alerts" panel is added to staff `/settings` (Instructors) writing `instructor_booking_alerts` (`saveBookingAlerts`), so `audit-settings-ui.py` shows a writer. Recorded as an amendment under Decision 33 in `docs/STUDIIOR_V1_DECISIONS.md` (before code). `test/instructor_alerts_test.sql` (UUID `a1e7`, 12 assertions): 6 bookings inside one window → ONE notice, headcount 6, "+6 booked"; a cancellation in a later window → a 2nd notice "1 cancelled" at headcount 5; an unassigned class → none; a draft month (studio with publication on) → none; the switch off → none; never another instructor's login. Teeth: replace the function with a `gen_random_uuid()` dedupe → 5 bookings become 5 notices. `all_off` canary carries a positive control (defaults → 0 alerts; switch on → exactly 1; off again). **ON HOSTED** (`20260831720000`, verified `remote`): drift IN STEP, anon surface exactly ELEVEN (unchanged), `queue_instructor_booking_alert`/`tg_instructor_booking_alert` reachable by neither client role, `change_line_text` `anon=false authed=true`, `set_email_each_booking` gone, `instructors.email_each_booking` dropped, `studio_settings.instructor_booking_alerts` present, null-guards IN STEP, audit shows the write path with no orphan. **Reform's switch is OFF until the owner turns it on in the UI** — a read-back is owed once they do.

- **166** `/book` gets the tenant's own text where members choose a class: a **dismissible free-first banner** and a **pinned-announcement strip**. **SUPERSEDED BY MIGRATION 168: the built-in free-first banner is removed (its `dismissFreeFirst` action and the `free_first_banner` key are gone) and the strip is now `kind='banner'`-only, not pinned-driven; the paragraph below describes the shape 166 shipped, not the current one.** The free-first banner (Decision 30) gains an X; the dismissal is a **`member_dismissals` row** (key `free_first_banner`), per member and durable across devices — **not localStorage** (per device) and not a `members` column (`guard_member_self_update` blocks a member writing one), so it is the `announcement_dismissals` shape generalised (`(member_id, key)`, self-scoped RLS, self-insert, no writer function). Once dismissed it stays dismissed; the "Book — first class free" row buttons keep carrying the offer while the member is eligible, so nothing is lost, and when eligibility ends the banner is gone anyway. The **announcement strip** renders the studio's **pinned**, published, in-range announcements (Decision 27, audience members/both) as a compact one-line row — title only, tap to expand the body, X to dismiss via the **existing `dismiss_announcement`**, so a dismissal on Book is a dismissal on Home too; **unpinned announcements stay Home-only** (the page filters `pinned`). Order on `/book`: announcement strip above the free-first banner. No new function, no anon change; `member_dismissals` is `anon_sel=true` by the hosted default with RLS the boundary (self-scoped, anon matches nothing), like `announcement_dismissals`. `test/announcements_test.sql` (now 22): the free-first dismissal is a row (persists; RLS self-scope — another member sees none; a member cannot dismiss for another, PT with-check); exactly one pinned announcement reaches the Book strip. **Browser-verified as an eligible lead with one pinned announcement** on a 375px viewport: both show (strip above the banner), the unpinned one is absent, tap expands the body, each dismisses independently, the free-first dismissal survives a reload (a server row, not storage) and the announcement dismissal propagates to Home; contrast 0 below floor, lowest 15.13. **ON HOSTED** (`20260831710000`, `remote` verified); full suite **58 clean, 0 failed**; `check-hosted-drift.sh` **IN STEP**; advisor anon **exactly eleven**; `check-null-guards.py` **IN STEP**.

- **165** Decision 34 Part B — the guest re-sign gate, the setup-checklist item, and the no-version state. **`enforce_guest_waiver`** (check-in trigger, re-issued from 132) now refuses a guest who signed only an OLDER version when the current one is `requires_resign`, exactly as it refuses an unsigned one — scoped to guests via the `guest_passes` join, so an ordinary member's check-in is untouched (their re-sign is the booking gate, `book_class` 2.1.4). **`studio_setup_state`** (re-issued from 155) gains a `waiver` item, present ONLY when `require_waiver` is on (like `publish` for publication): outstanding until a version is published, done once one is, absent when the switch is off; the checklist UI gets "Publish your waiver" → `/settings/features`. **THE NO-VERSION STATE:** `require_waiver` defaults ON, so a studio that requires a waiver but has published none must not send a member to an empty signing screen — **`book_class` rule 2.1.4 returns a distinct `waiver_unavailable`** (re-issued from 164, the newest, via `scripts/newest-definition.sh`) which `/book` renders as "This studio hasn't published its waiver yet — please ask the studio", the **Home banner says the same** (not-published / sign / re-sign are its three states — `current_waiver` re-issued to carry `require_waiver` so Home tells a waiver-requiring studio from an indifferent one), and `/waiver` already did. **A studio's existing bare-timestamp signatures still stand** (2.1.4 only re-refuses when a `requires_resign` version exists and was not signed), so Reform's signed members are unaffected — but an UNSIGNED member at Reform now gets `waiver_unavailable` until Reform publishes a waiver (the intended prompt). `test/waiver_test.sql` grows to 30: no-version → `waiver_unavailable`, then `waiver_not_signed` once published; the checklist item present/done/absent; a guest who signed an older required version refused check-in (PT422) and admitted after re-signing. **`book_class_test` now publishes a waiver version** for its `require_waiver`-on studio so an unsigned member gets `waiver_not_signed` not `waiver_unavailable` (the one suite the change touched — caught by the full run, 57 clean + this fixed). **ON HOSTED** (`20260831700000`, pushed this session, `remote` verified); full suite **58 clean, 0 failed**; `check-hosted-drift.sh` **IN STEP**; advisor anon **exactly eleven**; `check-null-guards.py` **IN STEP**.

- **164** Decision 34 Part A — the waiver becomes a real, versioned, signed document. Until now "signed the waiver" was a bare `members.waiver_signed_at` timestamp: no studio-supplied text (`studio_settings.waiver_text` was never read — now abandoned), nothing shown to the member (the Home banner was a one-tap button that signed nothing they had read), nothing recorded about what was agreed. **`waiver_versions`** (text or PDF, immutable — a new version supersedes, never edits — with a **content hash** and `requires_resign`) and **`waiver_signatures`** (version, hash copied from the version, the signature-image PATH not its bytes, the member-row NAME not client input, `signed_at`, `user_id`, `user_agent`, **best-effort request IP**, `method` app|paper). **NO SERVICE-ROLE CLIENT:** every existing write path to `member-documents` is desk-only, so 164 adds a **member-self storage INSERT policy** (`foldername[2]` = the member's own id, the `member-avatars` owner-write shape) and a SECURITY DEFINER **`sign_waiver_document`** that inserts the desk-only document + signature rows and sets `waiver_signed_at` as the owner (via the `studiior.waiver_signing` flag `guard_member_self_update` honours); the member's own Next session builds the signed PDF (pdf-lib) and uploads it + the signature PNG to their own folder. `record_document` stays the desk/paper path and now **records the version too** (`method='paper'`, no image). **`current_waiver`** is the member/staff reader (current version + whether this member signed it); **`set_waiver_version`** is the manager-up writer (text hashes its body; a PDF's hash is the file sha256 from the Next upload). **THE RE-SIGN GATE IS `book_class` RULE 2.1.4**, re-issued from migration **163** (proven the newest `book_class` by a SORTED grep of every definition before the drop — the same lowercase/uppercase-`public.` trap that bit 163): a member who signed only an older version is treated as unsigned when the current version is marked `requires_resign`; **a studio with no version yet is unaffected** (the current-version subquery finds nothing), so Reform's existing bare-timestamp signatures stand. **Signing screen** (`/waiver`, phone-first): the full content shown (text native, Sign disabled until scrolled to the end), a **drawn signature on a canvas** (pointer events, finger/stylus), the name **pre-filled from the member row and locked**; the signed PDF appends the signature block **at the end** (no field-placement editor) — signature image, name, date in the studio zone, version id, content hash. Staff editor in Settings → Member features (text or PDF + "require re-sign"). Home banner links to `/waiver` (the old inline `signMyWaiver` button is gone). `test/waiver_test.sql` (UUID `a17e`): publish → hash; self-sign → `waiver_signed_at` + a document + a signature carrying the version's hash and the member-row name; v2-no-resign stands; v3-requires-resign re-gates through `book_class` until re-signed; signing a non-current version refused PT409; the paper path records the version; a stranger reads neither another studio's waiver nor another member's signature. **Browser-verified on a 375px viewport with a REAL drawn stroke** end to end — the signed PDF's signature block carries the drawn signature, "Signed by Alena Fabricated", the studio-zone date, the version id and the content hash; DB confirms name/version/hash/UA/IP; signing-screen contrast 0 below floor, lowest 4.54. **ON HOSTED** (`20260831690000`, pushed this session, `remote` verified). Full suite **58 clean, 0 failed**; `check-hosted-drift.sh` **IN STEP**; advisor anon **exactly eleven** (`set_waiver_version`/`current_waiver`/`sign_waiver_document`/`book_class`/`record_document` all `anon=false authed=true`); the `waiver_versions`/`waiver_signatures` tables show `anon_sel=true` by the **hosted platform default exactly like `member_documents`/`memberships`/`notification_preferences` — RLS is the boundary and denies anon every row** (no anon-matching policy); `check-null-guards.py` **IN STEP**; `audit-settings-ui.py` no new orphan writer; guard probe on hosted — a genuine Reform member is refused `set_waiver_version` **PT403**. **Part B shipped in migration 165** (below the count).

- **163** Free-first opening fixes (from the November-opening trace). No behaviour change unless `free_first_class_enabled` is on. **ONE BOOKING PATH FOR AN ELIGIBLE MEMBER.** The `/book` list banner promised "no card, no plan" over an inline **Book** that called `book_class` — for a fresh lead that dead-ends at `waiver_not_signed`, and after they sign the waiver it silently books a paid **drop-in** and spends the free class. Now the inline Book (and the class-detail Book) route an eligible member to `book_first_free` and read **"Book free"**; the free path is never `book_class` for them. **THE BELT, in `book_class` rule 2.1.3b:** a self-serve **LEAD** who is free-first eligible is refused `use_free_first` before the waiver gate, so a direct API call (or a re-tap after signing) cannot spend the free class on a drop-in. **Gated on `status = 'lead'` and SELF-ONLY** (`v_is_self and not (v_is_desk or v_trusted or v_override)`): a front-desk booking of a walk-in lead still works (the desk cannot call the self-scoped `book_first_free`), and an active member with a plan — or a member bringing a guest through `book_guest`, which books their own seat here — is never a lead and books normally. **THE WRONG-BASE MISTAKE, caught by the suites:** the first draft rebuilt `book_class` from `20260830380000` because `grep … | tail -1` returned it in traversal order, not newest — that reverted the held-seat gate (126) and pending-payment drop-in, failing `waitlist_hold`/`stripe_connect`/`manual_payments`/`peak_allowance`/`platform_billing`/`publication`. `book_class` is re-issued from **`20260831310000` (waitlist seat hold — the genuine newest, confirmed by a SORTED grep of every definition)** with the belt inserted. **THE EMPTY STATE BEFORE OPENING:** a studio whose timetable starts weeks out (Reform: 9 Nov) showed `/book` empty days with "Try tomorrow", one tap at a time — `next_class_day` is staff-only. New **`member_next_class_day(studio, from)`** (member-guarded, scheduled + published only) drives **"Classes start {date}"** with a jump link. **OUT-OF-WINDOW CLASSES** render **"Opens for booking on {date}"** (`starts_at − booking_window_days`) instead of a Book button that refuses on tap — the publication horizon banner never covered this for a publication-off studio. **THE PLAN CATALOGUE:** `/account/plan` lists the studio's `visibility='public'` plans (name, price, includes) via the existing `plans_member_read` grant, under a **How-to-buy** line from new **`studio_settings.how_to_buy`** (nullable, default "Ask at the desk and we'll set you up", edited in Settings → Member features) plus the studio contact email — so "the plans are here" leads somewhere for a Decision-16 desk-sale studio. `member_bootstrap` (drop+recreate) gains `booking_window_days`, `how_to_buy`, `studio_contact_email`. **The `booking_confirmed` template gains a conditional free-first line** ("This one's on us — your first class is free."), set by `queue_booking_notifications` only for a comp booking carrying a host-null `guest_passes` row. `test/free_first_test.sql` grows to 36 (the belt: an eligible lead's `book_class` → `use_free_first`; an active member with a plan is NOT diverted; after the free class the ordinary path returns; the switch off never diverts; **a desk booking of an eligible lead is NOT refused and books**). Verified in the browser as a fresh lead: the free banner, **Book free** buttons, "Opens for booking {date}" on a 45-day class, "Classes start Monday 21 September" on a gap day, and the `/account/plan` catalogue with a custom how-to-buy and drop-in "Single class". **ON HOSTED** (`20260831680000`, pushed this session, `remote` verified). Full suite **57 clean, 0 failed**; `check-hosted-drift.sh` **IN STEP** (412 functions); advisor anon **exactly eleven** (unchanged — `book_class`/`member_bootstrap`/`member_next_class_day` all `anon=false authed=true`, `member_bootstrap` survived its drop+recreate authed-only); `check-null-guards.py` **IN STEP**; `audit-settings-ui.py` — `how_to_buy` has a write path, no new orphan.

- **162** Decision 33 Part B — the subscribable calendar feed, and the eleventh pre-login surface. Part A was the one-tap half (an .ics on a booking email); this is the feed a phone subscribes to once and then stays in sync on its own. **`calendar_feed(token)` IS THE ELEVENTH ANON SURFACE** — a calendar app subscribing carries no session, so the token in the URL is the whole authorisation (the stripe-webhook shape: the credential is the argument). It resolves a hashed token to a person and returns THEIR future classes as `text/calendar`: a member sees their own booked classes (UID = booking, via Part A's `ics_member_vevent`), an instructor their assigned classes (UID = occurrence, via `ics_instructor_vevent`, headcount in the body, SEQUENCE from `class_occurrences.updated_at` so a new booking re-syncs the count). **Scheduled + published only** (Decision 25 — a draft month is on nobody's calendar). Unknown or revoked token → null → the route 404s. Read-through cached 5 min in `calendar_feed_cache`, like `public_schedule`. **ONE .ics BUILDER** — the feed reuses Part A's assemblers; it adds no VEVENT construction, only two future-window queries. **THE TOKEN IS HASHED AT REST like `member_invites`**: `calendar_tokens` stores only the sha256, the raw token is returned once at mint (`encode(gen_random_bytes(24),'hex')`), a partial unique index per subject (`member_id`/`instructor_id where revoked_at is null`) keeps ONE live token per person, a re-mint revokes the old (the old URL dies), and revoke kills the feed. `mint_calendar_feed(studio,kind)` / `revoke_calendar_feed` / `calendar_feed_state` are authenticated and guarded per kind (a member by their own membership, an instructor by `auth_instructor_id` — RLS on `instructors` is manager-write, so they cannot flip it by hand). Both `calendar_tokens` and `calendar_feed_cache` are closed to every client role (the token_hash is a credential). **THE MONTH-ROSTER ATTACHMENT, deferred from Part A, lands here** on the shared multi-event builder `instructor_month_vevents(studio, instructor-or-null, month)` — `instructor_month_ics` (the button) now calls it, `notification_ics` gains a `month_roster` branch, and `publish_month` is re-issued **verbatim from 112** with two keys added to the roster payload (`instructor_id`, `month_ym`), so the roster email an instructor gets on publish carries their whole published month as one .ics. **Self-cancel is decided, not deferred:** a member's own cancel sends no email — the feed IS the sync channel, and a dropped booking simply leaves it. Next route `app/member/feed/[token]/route.ts` serves `text/calendar` (anon, no session); subscribe as `webcal://{slug}.studiior.app/feed/{token}`. Settings controls: a "Your calendar" block on the member `/settings` and the instructor `/instructor/me`, one shared `CalendarFeedControl` (token shown once, copy, Replace link, Turn off; the URL built from `window.location.host` so it is the studio's own subdomain). **The anon surface goes to ELEVEN** — `public_schedule_test`'s count assertion and the canonical list in this file both name `calendar_feed` in the same commit as the function; the migration itself asserts exactly eleven at apply time. `test/calendar_feed_test.sql` (UUID `feed`, 29 assertions): a member's feed carries their own booking and NOT another member's; an instructor's feed carries their assigned classes keyed by occurrence with no booking id leaking; a new booking raises the instructor event's SEQUENCE (cache cleared, as a real subscriber crosses the TTL); a revoked token and an unknown token both return null, and a re-mint gives a fresh working URL; a draft month is absent and appears once published; the `month_roster` notice's `notification_ics` is a multi-event VCALENDAR carrying the month's class; the anon surface is exactly eleven naming `calendar_feed`, both cache/token tables closed to anon; and a member cannot mint an instructor feed or a feed for a studio they are not in (PT403). Verified in the browser: the member `/settings` "Your calendar" control mints and reveals `webcal://…/feed/{token}` (shown once), the route returns `200 text/calendar` (member = 2 VEVENTs for her 2 future bookings, instructor = 44 for the seed), and an unknown token 404s. **ON HOSTED** (`20260831670000`, pushed this session, `remote` timestamp verified). **THE ADVISOR HAS BEEN RUN, both halves.** Grant half: the anon surface is **exactly eleven** — the ten plus `calendar_feed` (anon=true authed=true), no extras; `mint_calendar_feed`/`revoke_calendar_feed`/`calendar_feed_state`/`instructor_month_ics`/`publish_month` are `anon=false authed=true` (guarded), `instructor_month_vevents`/`notification_ics` are reachable by NEITHER client role, and `calendar_tokens`/`calendar_feed_cache` are closed to both (the token is a credential). `check-hosted-drift.sh` **IN STEP** (411 functions), `check-null-guards.py` **IN STEP**. Guard half, probed as the genuine test instructor (`4d74ce9e`, control ending in `raise` so nothing committed): `mint_calendar_feed(studio,'member')` refused **PT403** (she is not a member) and a foreign studio's `'instructor'` feed refused **PT403**, while her own `calendar_feed_state('instructor')` read without raising. **THE PRODUCTION ROUTE IS LIVE:** `https://reformcollective.studiior.app/feed/{token}` returns `200 text/calendar` — a real Reform instructor's token (Rhon Vince, 24 November classes) renders all 24 VEVENTs on production, so the Vercel route + hosted `calendar_feed` + hosted data all agree. The claimed test-login instructor (Deanna, `2202d1d7`) is assigned no future classes on hosted, so her feed is a valid but empty VCALENDAR until she is rostered.

- **161** Decision 33 Part A — the .ics builder, the attachment on booking emails, and the instructor per-booking opt-in. A web app cannot write to a phone's calendar silently; Part A is the one-tap half. **ONE .ics BUILDER, IN SQL** (`ics_escape`/`ics_fold`/`ics_prop`/`ics_dt` pure helpers, `ics_vevent`/`ics_calendar`, and the assemblers `ics_member_vevent`/`ics_instructor_vevent`/`ics_studio_location`) — used by the email pipeline, the Add-to-calendar buttons, and (Part B) the feed. A VEVENT is CRLF-folded, iCal-escaped, UTC-Z instants (no VTIMEZONE — a fixed single event is exact), `UID` = booking id (member) or occurrence id (instructor), `LOCATION` = studio address, `DESCRIPTION` = instructor first name + a Manage link (member) or the headcount (instructor). **`send_via_resend` gains the attachment 6 → 7 args by drop-and-recreate** (the 7th, `p_ics`, defaults null so the 6-arg callers — deliver + the notifications suite — still resolve to the one function; rebuilt from 143, only caller was `deliver_notification`; the migration asserts it is reachable by neither client role); `deliver_notification` builds the ics via **`notification_ics(id)`** (member VEVENT for `booking_confirmed`/`class_moved` PUBLISH, `class_cancelled` CANCEL; instructor VEVENT for `instructor_assigned`/`booking_for_instructor`) from the `occurrence_id` the queue functions now put in the payload, and passes it. **`booking_confirmed` gains a Manage-this-booking link** to the member class-detail (login required — no tokenised one-click cancel, a pre-login write). **SEQUENCE:** member event = `epoch(greatest(bookings.updated_at, class_occurrences.updated_at))` — a class MOVE touches the occurrence not the booking, so keying on the booking alone would leave `class_moved` at the same SEQUENCE and the calendar would ignore the update; instructor event = `epoch(class_occurrences.updated_at)`, which a booking bumps (the `booked_count` write fires `class_occurrences_updated` → `set_updated_at`), so a new booking re-syncs the headcount. **INSTRUCTOR PER-BOOKING EMAIL IS OPT-IN, OFF BY DEFAULT** (`instructors.email_each_booking`, their first notification preference — members have `notification_preferences`, instructors had none) — **SUPERSEDED BY MIGRATION 167: this per-instructor opt-in is dropped, replaced by the per-tenant `studio_settings.instructor_booking_alerts` switch (no instructor opt-out), coalesced one notice per class per 15-minute window; the paragraph below describes the shape 161 shipped, not the current one**: Reform at 21+ classes/week × 6 beds is 100+ "one person booked" emails a week, so the calendar's live headcount is the per-booking channel and email is not. `set_email_each_booking(studio, on)` is the self-guarded writer (RLS on `instructors` is manager-write, so an instructor can't set it directly); a toggle on `/instructor/me`. Buttons: member class-detail "Add to calendar" (route `/class/[id]/ics` → `member_class_ics`, guarded to the booking's owner), instructor My-schedule per-class + whole-month (`/instructor/ics/class|month/…` → `instructor_class_ics`/`instructor_month_ics`, the multi-event month respecting Decision 25 published-only). **THE GRANT TRAP, caught by the suites not by reading:** the re-issued `queue_instructor_assigned` and `queue_occurrence_cancelled` are INTERNAL (closed to `authenticated`) — `create or replace` keeps their ACL, and an explicit `grant … to authenticated` I added reopened them, failing `cover_and_commitment_test` and `notifications_test`; both are service-role only. Member-self-cancel still sends no email (decided, not deferred — the feed is the sync channel). `test/ics_test.sql` (UUID `ca1e`, 16 assertions): a booking → valid VEVENT (UID=booking, UTC DTSTART, SUMMARY); a move → same UID, higher SEQUENCE; a cancel → METHOD:CANCEL + STATUS:CANCELLED, same UID; the instructor event UID=occurrence and its SEQUENCE rises on a new booking; opt-in OFF → zero instructor emails, ON → exactly one to that instructor with an instructor .ics. Verified in the browser: the member "Add to calendar" link renders on a booked class and the route returns `200 text/calendar` with a well-formed VCALENDAR (UID = the member's booking). **The month_roster multi-event attachment is deferred to Part B**, where the feed's multi-event builder lives — the roster .ics IS the feed content for a month, and building it twice would be the two-implementations the decision forbids. **LOCAL: all suites pass via `run-suites.sh` (guarantee_contract's one full-run failure was transient — passes clean and via run-suites; the two real failures were the grant trap, fixed). Advisor/drift/null-guards/audit owed on hosted after push; anon surface stays exactly ten in Part A (`calendar_feed` is Part B).**

- **160** Decision 32 — the paid headcount is `greatest(booked_at_cutoff, booked_at_start)`, amending Decision 22. **THE CUTOFF COUNT ALONE UNDERPAID A LATE BOOKER.** The booking cutoff sits closer to start than the 12h pay cutoff, so a member can book after the pay cutoff and attend — a body the pay record never saw — and on a nearly-full class can take the last seat and never trigger the ₱1,400 full-house bonus. Migration `20260831650000` adds `class_occurrences.booked_at_start` and re-issues **`compute_class_pay_run`** (create-or-replace, ACL kept) so the RAN group branch reads `v_heads := greatest(coalesce(booked_at_cutoff,0), coalesce(booked_at_start,0))` and the basis records both counts + the one paid. **WHEN THE RECORD IS WRITTEN, AND WHY A SNAPSHOT IS NEEDED:** the pay record is written at the terminal transition (`tg_record_class_pay` on `committed_at` non-null), which for **core/flex is the cutoff, BEFORE start** — so `booked_at_start` does not exist at write time and `greatest` collapses to the cutoff. **`always` commits AT start** (its `cutoff_at = starts_at`), so its `booked_at_cutoff` is already the start count and it needs no snapshot. New **`snapshot_start_headcount(occurrence)`** (SECURITY DEFINER, service-role only — the grant is the boundary) stamps `booked_at_start` at start (counting the SAME statuses `evaluate_commitment` uses — `booked/attended/no_show/pending_payment`, so the two snapshots can never disagree) and trues up the OPEN-period record in place (`record_class_pay` is insert-and-skip, so the true-up updates directly); a **CLOSED period is never re-touched** — the difference is written as an **adjustment in the next open period** (Decision 22's correction mechanism, idempotent on `basis->>'true_up_of'`, note "late bookings after cutoff"). The true-up can only ever RAISE (`greatest` is monotonic in the start count), so the instructor never earns less than the cutoff promised and a late cancel after the cutoff cannot reduce pay. **`sweep_commitments` re-issued** (byte-for-byte from 081 + one added pass per studio) to run the snapshot at start — the same 15-min job that already commits `always` at start; service-role only, ACL kept. `pay_statement`/`pay_period_export` (re-issued from 159, ACL kept) carry a "2 at cutoff, 3 at start" note read from the basis, so the statement and CSV still agree line for line. **This is a COMPUTE change, not a rate change** — no new rate version; the rates are unchanged and only the headcount input moves; existing closed-period records stay as computed. Decision recorded in `docs/STUDIIOR_V1_DECISIONS.md` (amendment under Decision 22). `test/guarantee_pay_test.sql` grows to 118 (UUID `9a17`): #1 a late booker after the cutoff pays per-head (2→3, ₱875), #2 a late booker fills the last seat and triggers full house (5→6, ₱1,300), #3 a late cancel does not reduce pay (cutoff 4 wins over start 3), #4 no-shows still count at the start snapshot (4, not 0), #5 a start count lower than the cutoff pays the cutoff, the closed-period → adjustment path (original untouched, +₱75 in the next open period, idempotent), the statement/export note, and the sweep doing it end to end. **Teeth: revert `v_heads` to `booked_at_cutoff` alone → #1 and #2 fail (80000/102500 not 87500/130000), while #3–#5 hold on the floor and their recorded `booked_at_start` (stamped by the snapshot regardless of compute).** **All five affected suites pass via `run-suites.sh` (guarantee_pay 118, guarantee_contract, instructor_checkin, payroll_optional, all_off). ON HOSTED** (`20260831650000`, pushed this session, `remote` timestamp verified). **`check-hosted-drift.sh` IN STEP** (392 functions match), **`check-null-guards.py` IN STEP** (`snapshot_start_headcount` guards on `is_service_context`/`is_manager_up`, no bare `auth.uid()` equality). **THE ADVISOR HAS BEEN RUN:** the anon surface is **exactly ten** (unchanged); the create-or-replace re-issues kept their ACL — `compute_class_pay_run` is closed to both client roles (internal), **`snapshot_start_headcount` and `sweep_commitments` are reachable by NEITHER client role (service-role only — the new sweep pass is not a client surface, the grant is the boundary)**, and `pay_statement`/`pay_period_export` stayed `anon=false authed=true` (guarded). No new client-callable 056-shape surface, so no member probe is owed; `snapshot_start_headcount` is closed to clients (42501 no-grant), the internal-lost-no-guard shape. **OPEN, unchanged from Decision 31:** per-head "at the cutoff vs attending" — Decision 32 settles that pay counts BOOKINGS (greater of two booking snapshots), never attendance; the earlier note about attending-head pay is superseded by this, and Reform's payroll can go live on the greater-of rule.

- **159** Decision 31 — the settle date's second shape, plus the payroll config UI and instructor RATE entry. **THE PAYROLL CONTRACT WAS UNENTERABLE, AND NOW IS.** Migration `20260831640000` adds `studio_settings.pay_settle_offset_days` (days after a period's `ends_on`, 0 = the close date) beside migration 140's weekday `pay_settle_dow`; a CHECK enforces **exactly one shape** (`dow is null OR offset is null`) and bounds the offset `0..31`. `pay_settle_on()` is re-issued 3-arg with the offset branch, still pure/immutable — a **drop-and-recreate** (2-arg → 3-arg), so its ACL was re-asserted (`revoke from public, anon; grant to authenticated, service_role`); `pay_statement`/`pay_period_export`/`instructor_pay_summary` are **create-or-replace** (ACL kept) reading and passing the offset. So "the 15th and the last day of the month" = `semimonthly + second_day 16 + settle offset 0`, the last day coming from the period boundary (Feb resolves 28/29 with no special case). **UI closes SETTINGS_WITHOUT_UI's Decision-22 half:** a payroll settings screen (settle rule as not-set/weekday/offset with the offset-0 held-record caveat; `pay_period_anchor` shown only for weekly/fortnightly; a Conversion bonus block — switch, amount, window, read-only "Attributed to: the first class"), the plan form's `counts_for_conversion` checkbox (shown and written only while the bonus is on), and **an instructor RATE screen** on `/instructors/[id]` gated on `studio_uses_payroll()` — `set_instructor_rate()` had existed since 092 (guarded, granted, working) with no UI, so a studio's pay contract could not be entered; now a Pay section shows the latest rate (or "no rate on file — this instructor will not be paid"), the immutable effective-dated history, a new-version form (effective from tomorrow, never today), and **"copy this rate to all active instructors"** (one contract entered once, one version each through the same function, PT409-skip anyone who already has one). `audit-settings-ui.py` gains a generic check — every SECURITY DEFINER writer in `public` that a client can call yet nothing in `app/` names — which no longer flags `set_instructor_rate` and lists 20 mostly-internal/cron writers for review. **OPEN, before Reform's payroll goes live:** per-head pay is computed from `booked_at_cutoff`, so "₱75 per head at the cutoff" vs "per attending head" diverges for a late booker and must be settled (Decision 31, `compute_class_pay_run` cited). Tests: `test/pay_settle_test.sql` grows the offset/CHECK cases; `test/instructor_portal_test.sql` gains the rate create/supersede/PT409/PT403 assertions. **ON HOSTED** (`20260831640000`, pushed this session, `remote` timestamp verified). **THE ADVISOR HAS BEEN RUN, both halves; `check-null-guards.py` and `check-hosted-drift.sh` are IN STEP** (391 functions match). Grant half: the anon surface is **exactly ten** (unchanged); `pay_settle_on(date,int,int)` is `anon=false authed=true` and the old 2-arg is **gone** (the drop's re-asserted ACL held); the three re-issued readers and `set_instructor_rate` are all `anon=false authed=true`. Guard half PROVEN ON HOSTED, probed as instructor `2202d1d7` (control `is_manager_up=false  is_service_context=false`, block ending in `raise`): **`set_instructor_rate` refused PT403** (a non-manager cannot set a rate; the attempted write discarded), and with Reform's contract entered through the UI and its first period created via `ensure_pay_period` (`3571dc6c…`), **`pay_statement(another instructor)` refused PT403 while `pay_statement(self)` returned `ok=true settle_on=2026-09-30`** — the offset-0 pay date. **Reform's payroll is now live-configured on hosted** (semimonthly 16th/month-end, ₱900/₱75/2/₱1,400, conversion ₱500/30d on the recurring plans + packs), with the OPEN per-head-vs-attending question above still to settle before a period is actually paid.

Run the suites with **`scripts/run-suites.sh`** — one `db reset` per suite, because a shared reset lets one suite's leftovers decide another's result (the anon-surface count is the classic victim), and the runner waits for schema readiness so it cannot race its own reset.
- **158** Decision 30 — a new member's **first class is free**, per tenant, **off by default** (`studio_settings.free_first_class_enabled`; `free_first_peak_allowed` default true). It SHARES Decision 26's once-ever ledger rather than adding a second: a self-signup free class is a `guest_passes` row with **no host** (`host_member_id` made nullable), so the free-once key spans both doors. `free_first_eligibility` + `book_first_free` (a comp seat booked directly like a guest, no plan/card/credits, waiver at check-in not booking); reports split by host (`free_first_report`/`dashboard_free_first_kpi` are host-null, `guest_pass_report`/`dashboard_guest_kpi` re-scoped host-not-null). **`normalize_email_key()`** is the shared once-ever key (lower, trim, strip `+tags` for all, strip dots + fold `googlemail`→`gmail`), used ONLY in the free-once functional index and the `already_had_free`/`already_member` refusals of BOTH eligibility functions — never to address or link a person. **Two host-null arms fixed here after review:** `sweep_guest_waivers()` no longer aborts the whole cron for every tenant when a free-first pass is pending — it skips the (nonexistent) host nudge and runs each pass in its own subtransaction, recording a skip in `audit_logs` once per pass per day; and the staff roster shows a host-null pass as "First class (free)", not "Guest · Brought by a member". `guard_member_self_update`/`sign_waiver` gain a `studiior.waiver_signing` flag so a claimed member can sign their own waiver. `test/free_first_test.sql` (`f9ee`, incl. the Gmail-variant `already_member` at both doors, teeth-checked), `test/guest_waiver_sweep_test.sql` (`5eeb`, both isolation halves + the durable record, teeth-checked), and the `all_off` canary asserts no trace when off. Decision recorded in `docs/STUDIIOR_V1_DECISIONS.md` (## 30). **ON HOSTED** (`20260831630000`), advisor run both halves (see the 157-158 note above).**

Verified at 375. **Decision 29** (instructor claiming, migrations 149–156) is now written up in `docs/STUDIIOR_V1_DECISIONS.md`. **ON HOSTED** (`20260831620000`, applied earlier; advisor re-run this session — see the 157-158 note above).**
- **149–156** the claiming build and its follow-ons: **149** instructor claiming backend (per-tenant `claiming_enabled`, a soft per-instructor core weekly cap, `apply_for_shift` claiming-aware, `claim_ranking`/`instructor_claimable`); **150** the schedule surfaces publication state + a `publish` setup step (omitted when publication off); **151** `reassign_occurrence` — change/unassign from the calendar popover, both instructors told; **152** `public_schedule` window = the studio's `booking_window_days`, not a hardcoded fortnight; **153** claiming works WITHOUT publication (`month_published` is true when the switch is off, so no reveal step) — `instructor_claim_horizon` walks the occurrence horizon by month, the settings toggle forces NO publication, the portal reads the horizon; **154** the claim screen as an Apple-Calendar month grid (accent from `lib/theme.ts`; core dot = ink filled, flex ring = ink hollow, mine = `--lime-text`; over-cap amber fixed) + `claim_guarantee_terms` (the footnote read live from settings + rate version) + `week_core` on `instructor_claimable`; **155** B — narrowing a standing pattern no longer silently strands a class: `availability_conflicts(studio)` + `set_instructor_availability_rows` detects and notifies staff (a list on `/shifts/applications`) and the instructor; **156** cover auto-accept (per-tenant `cover_auto_accept_enabled`, reuses `cover_escalation_hours`, cap-exempt, `accept_cover`/`cover_available_to`) and `request_cover` records a claim-then-drop for `instructor_reliability`. Tests: `test/claiming_test.sql` (`c1a1`), `test/cover_auto_test.sql` (`c0e5`). **149–156 ARE ALL ON HOSTED** (`20260831540000`–`20260831610000`, verified with `supabase migration list --linked`; 156 landed after the first advisor pass). **DRIFT (the 070/075 fault) was NOT found** — the drift once reported (`set_instructor_availability_rows` differing, `availability_conflicts` hosted-only) was local LAGGING hosted before 155 was applied locally; with 155 in both they are byte-identical (`7f88dd9d…`, `12ba6f8d…`), so no fix-forward migration was written, and once 156 was pushed `request_cover`/`accept_cover`/`cover_available_to` all matched local too (`dc46019d…`, `aa29b6f9…`, `ce7c8fbc…`). `check-hosted-drift.sh` clean. **ADVISOR RUN AGAINST HOSTED FOR 153–156, both halves.** Grant half: the anon surface is **exactly ten** (unchanged — nothing 153–156 added an eleventh). It is wired to instructor `2202d1d7` (`instructor_user_id` returns the login). Probed as that instructor (control `role=authenticated  auth.uid=4d74ce9e  auth_instructor_id=2202d1d7  is_manager_up=false  is_service_context=false`, block ending in `raise`): `instructor_claim_horizon`, `claim_guarantee_terms`, `instructor_claimable` (and `instructor_week` and the three 620 notification functions) each refused ANOTHER instructor with **PT403** and RETURNED for themselves.


- **148** `public_schedule` looks ahead, and says WHICH empty it is. 147 returned a studio's classes for the next N days, but Reform Collective's timetable starts 9 November — eight weeks out — so the embed on their own site showed a blank box, and "no classes in the window", "the month is not published", "unknown slug" and "a bad key" all rendered identically (an hour to diagnose). Two changes, both in the payload. **LOOK AHEAD** — the window is a deliberate HYBRID: the primary window is the next N DAYS (right for a busy studio — shows the week), and when that is empty the fallback is the next N CLASSES (`limit 40`, right for a studio opening, sparse, or publishing a month ahead — real classes wherever they fall, not a fixed empty span). **NAME THE STATE** — `state` is `in_window` / `upcoming` (with `next_from`, the classes returned) / `unpublished` (scheduled classes exist ahead but their month is not published — the studio must publish) / `empty` (nothing ahead); `found:false` for an unknown slug. The embed renders each distinctly: upcoming shows "Classes start {date}" above the week; unpublished says "This studio has not published a timetable yet" (the case a studio pasting the embed before publishing must be able to read); `found:false` and any non-2xx (a bad key / project URL is 401 on hosted, confirmed) or fetch failure say "Could not load the schedule" — never the same blank box as an empty studio. **A null instructor is OMITTED, never "TBC"** (Decision 17): the endpoint already returns `instructor_first_name: null` for an unassigned class, the embed's `if (c.instructor_first_name)` drops the line, and the member app already omits it too (the book card's `[instructor, room].filter(Boolean)`, the class-detail `{instructor && …}`) — so publishing November unassigned advertises no uncertainty. `create-or-replace` kept the ACL; anon surface unchanged (still ten). `test/public_schedule_test.sql` grows to 35 (UUID `ec40`). **LOCAL until pushed; advisor owed on hosted after push (grant surface unchanged, but confirm anon stays ten).**

- **147** `public_schedule(slug)` — the TENTH pre-login surface, and the first new anon surface since 097. A studio embeds its own timetable on its Webflow marketing site; a visitor sees this week's real classes and clicks through to the member app to book. It is ANON, so it is held to the nine's discipline and exposes ONLY what a stranger may see: branding (accent/theme/logo — the same fields `studio_by_slug` feeds the member app) and, per class, date/time/duration/name/description/class-type colour/room/instructor FIRST NAME + photo/spaces-left-or-full/a `book_url` into the member app. **PUBLISHED MONTHS ONLY** (Decision 25, via `month_published`) — an unpublished future month is not bookable and is absent. `status='scheduled'` only, which excludes a cancelled AND a not-running flex class, exactly as the member app. **No member data, no bookings, no names**; spaces derived from `capacity - booked_count`, clamped to 0. Instructor first name via `split_part(display_name,' ',1)`; NO surname, bio or contact. An unknown/inactive slug returns `{found:false}` and writes nothing. **CACHE IT** (the only endpoint a stranger can hammer): a read-through cache table `public_schedule_cache` (5-min TTL), written by the SECURITY DEFINER function alone (RLS on, grants revoked from every client role) — a repeat hit is one indexed read, not the joins. The embed is `embed/schedule-embed.html`, a self-contained script the studio pastes into a Webflow embed block: it fetches by slug, brands itself with the studio's OWN accent/logo (read live, so changing the accent changes the embed), and shows a Mindbody-style **day strip** (seven days, date above/weekday below, today marked, week arrows) over **rows with air** — time+duration, room, a large circular instructor photo (initials-ring fallback: the instructor's initial when assigned-with-no-photo, the studio's when unassigned, since a null instructor is omitted not "TBC"), class name + first name stacked, and "Reserve"/"Waitlist Only"+"Join Waitlist" on the right. **The hand-off is the SAME block reacting to a `?studiior_class={id}` query param** (pushState, so browser back/forward and shared links work — the studio pastes ONE thing): clicking a class shows a reserve page on the studio's own site — Back to Schedule, class name, day/date/time in the studio zone, `room · class · duration · instructor`, View class details → `book_url`, then "Log in to continue" with **LOG IN** → `{app_origin}/login?next=/class/{id}` and **Create one now** → `{app_origin}/signup?next=/class/{id}`. No auth, no forms in the embed. Every colour derives from the accent and clears 4.5:1 (the LOG IN fill darkens until white text on it passes; the "Today"/back marks use the accent's text step); verified at 375/768/1440 with zero DOM overflow. NO Studiior branding. **THE EMBED IS IMMUNE TO THE HOST PAGE'S CSS, and a clean harness cannot prove it** — the first version set `font-family:inherit` with single-class selectors, so a Webflow host's serif body, condensed uppercase headings and `content-box` flowed straight in on the LIVE site (class names uppercased, the daybar date crushed to overlapping glyphs that read as "two dates in one box"). Fixed by hard-scoping EVERY rule under the mount's id (`#studiior-schedule …`, an id beats a host's class/element selectors on specificity), a full reset on every descendant (font/weight/letter-spacing/line-height/text-transform/box-sizing/margins/borders/background), and explicit typography re-asserted on every text element. Clicking a class no longer scrolls (it re-renders in place; browser-back restores scroll via default restoration + the synchronous popstate re-render), and a `data-sd-init` guard stops a double-paste rendering twice. **Verify inside a page with its own aggressive typography, never a clean harness** — the bleed-through only appears against a hostile host. `test/public_schedule_test.sql` (UUID `ec40`, 26 assertions). **ON HOSTED** (`20260831520000`), verified with `supabase migration list --linked`. **THE ADVISOR HAS BEEN RUN AGAINST HOSTED.** Grant half clean: the anon surface is now **exactly ten** — `accept_studio_invite, claim_instructor_account, claim_member_account, instructor_invite_preview, member_invite_preview, public_schedule, stripe_platform_webhook, stripe_webhook, studio_by_slug, studio_invite_preview` — no extras, none absent; `public_schedule` is `anon=true authed=true` and the `public_schedule_cache` table is closed to anon (`select=false, insert=false`). The DATA boundary (no member data, first-name-only instructor, published+scheduled only) is proven LOCALLY by the suite — it **could not be exercised on hosted** because `reformcollective`'s timetable is in November and the public window is 14 days, so an anon call returns `found=true` with **0 classes** for now; that is the "hosted has no data in range yet" honesty, not a pass claimed as if the rows were there. Nothing to null-guard (`public_schedule` is a deliberate public read, guarded by grant + slug resolution, not by a member check).

- **146** re-notify qualified instructors about an open shift. An unassigned class is ALREADY an open shift — created with no instructor it gets `staffing='open'` (`tg_derive_staffing`), which stamps `shift_opened_at`, and `notify_open_shifts()` emails every qualified instructor ONCE (keyed on `shift_alert_sent_at`). There is no separate "publish" state to reach and no way to ask again once that one alert went out. `republish_open_shift(occurrence)` (manager-up, future/scheduled/open only) clears `shift_alert_sent_at` so the next sweep re-solicits — the honest action-form of "put it out to instructors" for a shift that is already open, a nudge not a first publish. Returns the count of qualified instructors WITH A LOGIN (the ones `notify_open_shifts` can actually email; Reform's have none, so it says so rather than implying a message). This backs the roster's "Email qualified instructors again" beside the new Assign control. Assertions folded into `test/shift_ops_test.sql` (UUID `0b57`). **ON HOSTED** (`20260831510000`) — see the 144–146 advisor note below.
- **145** the core holding fee is a PAIR, and either half may be blank. 138 gave core a flat slot-holding fee (`core_unmet_pay_cents`, nullable) beside the percentage, resolved as alternatives — `coalesce(core_unmet_pay_cents, base * coalesce(core_unmet_pay_pct, 0) / 100)`, flat wins. But `core_unmet_pay_pct` was NOT NULL DEFAULT 50, so a studio paying a flat ₱400 with no percentage could not store it: the settings form REQUIRED the percentage and refused the blank its own help text documented. Drop NOT NULL so the two are true alternatives (null pct = "no percentage", mirror of the nullable flat); the compute already coalesces a null pct to 0, the DEFAULT of 50 stays so the all-off canary and defaults sweep are unaffected, and the range CHECK passes on null. The form no longer marks the percentage `required`, and the action validates the PAIR — at least one set, only when guarantees is on, both-blank the one refusal — and posts a hidden flat when the panel is collapsed (a latent wipe: collapsing core had nulled a flat fee the studio set). Schema only; no function re-issued. **ON HOSTED** (`20260831500000`); schema-only, so no function surface to advise on.

- **144** a one-off class can carry a guarantee tier, like a series. `create_occurrence()` (089, the shared create gate) always made a class core — it took no tier, so a Saturday workshop or a cover class could not be set to run regardless (always) or only if booked (flex). Gains `p_tier` + `p_min_bookings`, set the `set_series_guarantee()` way (flex → flex=true + minimum_bookings; core → core_min_bookings; a flex minimum falls back to the studio's `flex_min_bookings`). **Adding two params with defaults ADDS AN OVERLOAD, it does not replace** (028's trap) — so the 7-arg signature is DROPPED first and the ACL re-asserted. **THE STANDALONE-FLEX WARNING**: a one-off flex class usually has no class of the same instructor beside it — the standalone case `occurrence_is_adjacent_run()` flags and the instructor agreement pays standby for — so a flex one-off that lands standalone comes back with a `standalone_flex` warning (only where flex is actually on, so an inert flex tier at a flex-off studio says nothing). A warning, never a refusal; the one-off analog of `standalone_count`. **The one-off FORM now goes through `create_occurrence()` too** — it had been a bare INSERT since forever (the exact gap 089 closed for the calendar but not the form), so it also gains the validity/clash checks; and the warning can ONLY come from the function because `occurrence_is_adjacent_run()` is closed to clients. UI: a shared `components/tier-field.tsx` (inline radios + min, gated on guarantees/flex like the series control, absent otherwise) on both the one-off form (`/classes/new`) and the calendar's click-to-create; the `standalone_flex`/`outside_availability` warnings surface in the success line. `test/one_off_tier_test.sql` (UUID `0f17`, 12 assertions). **ON HOSTED** (`20260831490000`).

**144–146 ARE ON HOSTED** (`20260831490000`–`20260831510000`), verified with `supabase migration list --linked` (all three carry remote timestamps; last remote `20260831510000`). **The claim was made twice before it was true** — the first two times the list showed `remote=""` and the hosted schema still had the 7-arg `create_occurrence` and no `republish_open_shift`, so the advisor was NOT run and nothing was fabricated; the third time it was genuinely pushed and the run below is real. **THE ADVISOR HAS BEEN RUN AGAINST HOSTED FOR 144 AND 146, both halves, and `check-null-guards.py` reports IN STEP.** Grant half: the anon surface is **exactly the nine** pre-login functions (no extras, none absent); **`create_occurrence`'s drop-and-recreate did NOT reopen it to anon** — it is `anon=false authed=true` with a single 9-arg signature (no 7-arg overload survived the drop), because 144 re-asserts its own `revoke … from public, anon; grant … authenticated`; `republish_open_shift` is `anon=false authed=true`. Guard half — both are the 056 shape (take ids, WRITE) and both guard `is_manager_up` before any write. Probed as the genuine hosted Reform member (slug is `reformcollective`, one member with a login and not staff; control `role=authenticated auth.uid_set=t is_manager_up=f is_service_context=f`, the whole `do` block ending in `raise` so nothing committed — both WRITE): **`create_occurrence` refused PT403 and `republish_open_shift` refused PT403**, each before writing, and hosted was unchanged afterward. 145 is schema-only (nullable `core_unmet_pay_pct`), no function surface.


- **143** the instructor invite reached Resend with `"to": [null]` and 422'd — six instructors blocked for a week, the portal with no users. `invite_instructor()` queues through `queue_notification()`, whose recipient is a MEMBER (it inserts `recipient_type='member', member_id =` the 2nd arg); an invitee has no member row and no login, so it passes `null` there, and `render_notification`'s member lookup and staff branch both resolved to null. The address the invite was SENT TO is in the payload as `to_email` — the only address that exists at that point — and the renderer never read it. **Fix A:** `render_notification`'s `to_email` is now `coalesce(nullif(payload->>'to_email',''), v_staff_email, m.email)` — the payload wins, so an invite (or any hand-addressed row) reaches its recipient; harmless for every other notification (none set it). **Fix B:** `instructor_invite` joins the no-settings-link footer branch — an invitee has no account, so "Choose which emails you get → member-app `/settings`" was the wrong building; it now reads "you are getting this because {studio} set up your account", like `member_invite`. **Fix C:** a null recipient is refused with a reason BEFORE the provider — `queue_notification` raises PT422 when `member_id` is null AND the payload carries no `to_email` (the only null-member caller is the invite, which always has one, so it never fires for valid calls); `deliver_notification` raises PT422 if the rendered `to_email` is null, and the send worker marks that ONE row failed with the reason rather than posting a null (`queue_shift_notice` already skipped a null user). **Watch the rebuild-from-the-newest-file rule:** the first draft copied `deliver_notification` from migration 030 and reverted its `send_via_resend` call from six args to five (034 added `reply_to`) — caught by `notifications_test`, fixed to the 034 signature. Four assertions folded into `test/instructor_portal_test.sql` (UUID `beef`): the invite renders to its sent-to address, the footer offers no settings link, and both guards raise PT422. **REMEDIATION after push: resend the six invites** (`invite_instructor` supersedes the dead link) — the failed notification rows do not auto-retry. **ON HOSTED** (`20260831480000`), verified with `supabase migration list --linked`. **ADVISOR RUN — grant half clean, and there is no guard half to run:** `render_notification`, `queue_notification` and `deliver_notification` are all `anon=false authed=false` on hosted (closed to both client roles — `create or replace` kept the ACL migration 033/034 set), the anon surface is still exactly the nine, and because these three are closed to clients there is no client-callable 056-shape surface to probe as a member (the grant is the boundary, not a guard). `check-null-guards.py` reports **IN STEP** (queue/deliver guard on a null recipient, not on `auth.uid()`).


- **142** the optionality gates the `all_off` canary surfaced, and seat caps' missing predicate. Three instructor-OBLIGATION emails fired for a studio that opted into nothing — a confirmation request implies an obligation to a feature the studio does not run — and each is now gated the record_class_pay way, off by default. **`week_confirm_enabled` DEFAULT flipped true→false**: it was NOT NULL DEFAULT true, so every provisioned studio had weekly confirmation on and `sweep_week_confirmations` asked its instructors-with-logins each week. The default was the whole bug (the sweep already reads the stored value), so no re-issue — existing rows keep their value (only Reform, whose instructors have no logins), new/untouched studios get nothing. **Availability reminders had NO switch at all** — `sweep_availability_reminders` looped every active studio unconditionally; added `availability_reminders_enabled` (default false) and gated the sweep's loop on it (the availability EDITOR stays open; only the automated nudge becomes opt-in). **`sweep_instructor_confirmations`** (pay check-in) re-issued with an inline `guarantees_enabled OR flex_enabled` gate — already emergently inert (held pay records only exist with payroll) but now payroll-only by statement. **`studio_uses_seat_caps(studio)`** — the one predicate the app asks instead of null-checking `plan_seats()` per surface; `plan_seats`/`plan_seats_taken` are UNCHANGED (they already return no rows when the switch is off, 102), only the gating moved.
