The plan form/new-plan pages derive `seatCaps` from it; a Weekly-confirmation and Availability-reminders toggle join the instructors settings panel (a studio that WANTS weekly confirmation now turns it on, since it defaults off). The `all_off` canary is strengthened to a login instructor with an in-window class on the ask day, so "0 notifications from the sweeps" now means the switch, not the absence of somebody to email — with a positive-control teeth (flip weekly confirmation on → that instructor IS asked). `test/all_off_test.sql` (UUID `0ff0`, now 23). **LOCAL until pushed; advisor + `check-null-guards.py` owed on hosted (both run clean locally).**


- **141** G — the roster deadline and CARRY-FORWARD ON SILENCE. Publishing a month (112) sends each instructor their roster and confirming it (113) is one press; this is what happens when they say NOTHING. Past a deadline the studio sets (`roster_confirm_days`, default 5) from `notified_at`, a silent instructor's LAST month is carried into THIS one so the month is staffed rather than left open because somebody missed a button. **OFF BY DEFAULT, PER TENANT** (`carry_forward_enabled`, false): the sweep skips a studio with the switch off, the preview reports disabled, nothing is assigned — the teeth check turns it off and the same silent instructor carries nothing. **The match is WEEKDAY + LOCAL CLOCK TIME + CLASS TYPE, never series id.** **Only ASSIGNED AND CONFIRMED, not merely assigned**: the source is last month's classes that were theirs AND that they confirmed the month for (`roster_confirmations.confirmed_at` not null) AND did not hand back through `request_cover` (a pending/approved cover request excludes the class entirely). A month that was ITSELF carried on silence is NOT a confirmed baseline (`carried_at`, distinct from `confirmed_at`) — silence carries once from a real yes and does not propagate. **What does not carry, and the studio is told why**: an archived (or deleted) class type, a MOVED source slot (`is_exception` — a one-off, not a standing class), a pattern with no matching open class this month, a slot already staffed. **What carries WITH A WARNING**: a slot now outside the instructor's stated availability — still carried (the studio chose to lean on last month), flagged. **Assigned by a direct guarded UPDATE into an OPEN slot only**, NOT `move_occurrence()` (which hard-blocks outside the availability window — and carrying-with-a-warning is the whole point); an already-assigned slot is never overwritten and the instructor exclusion still refuses a clash. `roster_carry_plan` (internal, the one deterministic planner both preview and apply read), `roster_carry_due`, `roster_carry_preview` (manager-up), `carry_forward_roster` (manager-up or service, idempotent — a carried row is skipped, marks `carried_at`, audits `roster.carried_forward` with the report), `sweep_roster_carry` (service-role, cron `studiior-roster-carry :: 20 3 * * *`). UI: a carry-forward toggle + deadline in `/settings` (Instructors), and the preview + a "Carry forward now" button on `/publish` showing every silent instructor and what will and will not carry, with why. `test/roster_carry_test.sql` (UUID `c4f0`, 20 assertions). **On hosted; advisor run both halves clean, `check-null-guards.py` IN STEP.**
- **140** F — the PAYMENT DATE ("Friday after close"). Decision 22 computes what is owed and never moves money, but an instructor reading a closed statement still asked "so WHEN do I get it". A studio names a settle day (`studio_settings.pay_settle_dow`, 0=Sun..6=Sat, `extract(dow)` convention) and every statement carries the actual date — the first such day STRICTLY AFTER the period's `ends_on` (a period ending on a Friday, settled Fridays, pays the NEXT Friday, not the same day). Pinned to `ends_on`, not to when a manager got round to closing, so the promise is stable whether closed on time or late. **OFF BY DEFAULT, PER TENANT**: `pay_settle_dow` is nullable, null = no date shown. `pay_settle_on(ends_on, dow)` is pure IMMUTABLE date math (no tenant read, no guard); `pay_statement`, `pay_period_export` and `instructor_pay_summary` read the studio's own dow and surface `settle_on` (all re-issued with create-or-replace, so ACL kept). UI: a Payment-day control in the payroll settings, the date on the instructor's My Pay (until actually paid) and the staff period detail. `test/pay_settle_test.sql` (UUID `5e77`, 11 assertions). **On hosted; advisor run, grant half clean.**

- **139** payroll is OPT-IN, and a leak found by proving it. Instructor pay IS the guarantee system (Decision 22): a class that RAN only pays via `committed_at`, set only when guarantees/flex are on. But the CANCELLED path was gated differently — `tg_record_class_pay` fired `record_class_pay` for ANY cancelled class, and `record_class_pay` writes whenever a rate version exists (its only skip is "no rate on file"). So a studio that never turned guarantees on but had a rate version (by hand, or carried in by an import) accrued instructor pay on a `studio_fault`/closure cancellation — a cost it never opted into (reproduced: guarantees-off + a stray rate + a studio_fault cancel → 1 record). `tg_record_class_pay` re-issued to gate the cancelled path on `guarantees_enabled OR flex_enabled`, matching the committed path; a guarantees-off studio now generates NOTHING, rate version or not. `studio_uses_payroll(studio)` is the one predicate the app gates its Pay surfaces on — the staff rail's Pay (via `screen.tsx`/`railGroups`) and the instructor portal's Pay tab (via `instructorScreen`); a studio paying in its own books shows no Pay at all, absent not empty. **Reform's ladder is ONE studio's contract, not a product rule.** `core_unmet_pay_pct` still defaults to 50 — that is the holding-fee default for a studio that DOES turn guarantees on, not a cost imposed on a non-opted-in one, so it is left (flagged for a product call on whether the amount too should be explicit). `test/payroll_optional_test.sql` (UUID `0b71`).
- **138** the guarantee contract, cross-checked against Reform Collective's signed instructor agreement. Four gaps, all PER TENANT (a studio with guarantees off sees none of it). **A** a FLAT slot-holding fee beside the percentage — `studio_settings.core_unmet_pay_cents`, and `compute_class_pay_run`'s group core not_running branch reads `coalesce(flat, base*pct/100)`, so a flat amount wins where set and the percentage is the fallback (50% of 900 is 450, not 400; "pay 400 regardless" cannot be a percentage of a rate that changes). **C** the CORE early latch — `class_occurrences.core_reached_minimum_at` + `tg_core_reached_minimum` (AFTER UPDATE OF booked_count) + a `core_committed` notice, mirroring flex's `flex_reached_minimum_at` EXACTLY, fired when a core class first reaches its minimum (first booking). **NOTIFICATION ONLY**, and that is the one thing it must not copy from flex: `evaluate_commitment` does not read it, so `committed_at` and `booked_at_cutoff` stay written at the cutoff — a core class empty at the cutoff is still `not_running` and pays the slot-holding fee (the contract's own pay table), where committing it early would overpay (₱900 not ₱400) and mislabel. **B** post-commitment reassurance — `tg_notify_committed_cancel` (AFTER UPDATE OF status on bookings) queues `booking_cancelled_committed` with the unchanged pay when a member cancels a COMMITTED, still-scheduled class; not for a non-committed one, not during a studio release (the releasing flag). **D** a standalone-flex WARNING — `set_series_guarantee` (and `set_series_flex`) return `standalone_count` (flexed occurrences with nothing of the instructor's beside them, a standby-fee obligation), surfaced in the tier control so it is not created silently; a warning, never a refusal, since adjacency is dynamic. UI: a flat-fee field in `/settings` guarantees. **`instructor_no_show` is deliberately NOT a cancellation cause** — Decision 28's held-until-confirmed check-in is how a no-show is represented; a fourth cause would let a class read `instructor_no_show` while its pay record sits confirmed, the two disagreeing (recorded in `docs/STUDIIOR_V1_DECISIONS.md`). `test/guarantee_contract_test.sql` (UUID `9c07`).
- **137** the payroll CSV export. `pay_period_export(period)` returns `pay_statement`'s exact line shape across EVERY instructor in the period, plus a per-instructor summary — same source (`instructor_pay_records` + occurrences), same status words ("ran" / "did not run" / "cancelled — cause"), same categories (taught / not run / cancelled / bonus / adjustment). Manager-up. **An export must reflect a closed period exactly as the statement does** — if the CSV and the statement disagree, the CSV is what an accountant believes and the studio has a problem — so the numbers are the database's and the app (`/pay/[periodId]/export/route.ts`) only formats them into `text/csv`. The suite asserts export ↔ statement parity three ways (row count, total, and each line's amount) for a closed period.
- **136** proof that a studio paid an instructor. Studiior does NOT move money (Decision 22 stands); this records a payment made elsewhere — bank transfer, GCash, cash — so the instructor's statement reads paid and "did you pay me" stops. `instructor_pay_settlements` (studio, period, instructor, paid_on, method, reference, proof_path, recorded_by; unique per period+instructor) + a PRIVATE per-studio bucket `instructor-pay-proofs` (5 MB, png/jpeg/webp/pdf), path `<studio>/<instructor>/<file>` so the storage read policy lets an instructor reach only their own receipt (`auth_instructor_id(studio) = the folder's instructor segment`). RLS on the table: managers read all, an instructor reads their own. `record_pay_settlement(period, instructor, paid_on, method, reference, proof_path)` — manager-up, upsert, audited `pay_settlement.recorded`, and **refuses an OPEN period (PT409): you pay what the closed statement says.** UI: staff `/pay` (period list + the unconfirmed banner, a per-instructor detail with held amounts and a mark-paid form), "Pay" in the manager rail, and the instructor's My Pay showing paid status/date and a signed-URL receipt.
- **135** Decision 28 — instructor check-in for pay. Decision 22 wrote the pay records but nothing recorded that the instructor was in the room. THE INSTRUCTOR CHECKS THEMSELVES IN, in the portal on My week, one tap, only within a window (the member check-in window's own settings, migration 007, reused not hardcoded). A ran class's pay record is written HELD (`confirmed_at` null); `instructor_confirm_class()` (self, in-window) stamps it confirmed method `self`; a not-running class is auto-confirmed (`auto`), needing no check-in. **A CLOSED period cannot contain a held record** — `close_pay_period()` refuses while any class record is held, naming the count (PT409). Staff release a held one with a reason via `confirm_class_for_pay()` (manager, method `manager`, audited `pay_record.released`). `sweep_instructor_confirmations()` (cron */30) reminds. `studio_unconfirmed_pay_count()` for the banner. `record_class_pay`, `pay_statement` (per-line confirmed/payable + confirmed/held totals), `instructor_week` (checked_in + checkin_open) and `instructor_pay_summary` (a latent bug fixed: it read basis columns that don't exist) all re-issued. New suite `test/instructor_checkin_test.sql` (UUID `9a4d`).
- **134** payroll frequency. Decision 22's periods were fortnightly-by-default and days-based, with NO control to set the frequency — the suspension/horizon gap again. `studio_settings.pay_period_mode` (`weekly` / `fortnightly` / `monthly` / `semimonthly`, default fortnightly so every existing period is unchanged) + `pay_period_second_day` (the twice-monthly split, default 16). `ensure_pay_period()` branches: monthly = calendar month, semimonthly = 1..(second-1)/second..end, weekly = 7 days, fortnightly = 14 — all in studio-local dates, never intervals on instants. UI: a Payroll block in `/settings` (mode + second-day) and "current period closes on X".
- **133** a latency fold: `guest_passes_enabled` and `has_payment_provider` (the "connected provider" concept, `stripe_account_id is not null`) move into `member_bootstrap`, so no member screen makes a separate `studio_member_settings` round trip for them. Measured the member app after the Account/announcements work: nothing had gained a new serial DB hop (Home's new reads were already batched), but the class-detail sheet was 3 serial hops (bootstrap -> occurrence -> a batch that included a `studio_member_settings` call) and `/account/pay` and `/account/plan` each made their own settings call. Folding the two flags into the bootstrap (drop+recreate of the RETURNS TABLE) lets the class sheet take a `class_type_id` hint from the list card and resolve in ONE wave (2 hops, down from 3), and drops a query from `/account/pay` and `/account/plan`. The `/account` hub also got the plan-and-credits glance back at the top (it had turned a one-tap "what are my credits" into two). No schema change beyond the bootstrap signature.
- **132** Decision 27 — studio announcements. A studio posts something (workshop, closure, new instructor, price change, event) and members see it on Home. `announcements` (title, body, focal-point photo like 116, starts/ends, draft/published, audience members/instructors/both, pinned, notified_at) + `announcement_dismissals`. **ONE-WAY, never a feed** — Decision 13 excluded posts/comments/likes and that stands; no replies, no reactions. **Optional BY EXISTENCE, no switch**: no published-in-range announcement -> no "What's on" section on Home (the challenge pattern); the staff admin is always in the manager's rail (a basic tool), member visibility is pure existence. Staff CRUD (`create/update/publish/unpublish/delete_announcement`, `staff_announcements`); `member_announcements` (published, in range, audience members/both, not dismissed, pinned-first) and `instructor_announcements` (instructors/both, portal, no dismissal); `dismiss_announcement`. Publishing offers opt-in notify (default off — emailing every announcement trains members to ignore them), keyed on the announcement, never re-sent, members only. UI: a dismissible Home section, a staff `/announcements` list+form+edit with cover focal upload (studio-branding bucket), the instructor portal section. Verified in the browser: the announcement renders on Home (pinned, no dismiss) and in the staff admin.
- **131** the member Account rebuild's reads. `studio_member_settings` gains `has_payment_provider` (a generic "connected provider" concept — Decision 16's adapter idea — implemented as `stripe_account_id is not null`, so PayMongo/Xendit slot in later with no UI change; a drop+recreate of the RETURNS TABLE). `member_milestones(studio)` — the recognition standing LEADING with the next target (Decision 10: personal, no ranking): total lifetime visits, the next rung from `milestone_visit_targets()`, how far, and the ladder with each rung marked earned. (Members already read their own `payments` via the `payments_self` policy, so no migration was needed there.)
- **130** the null-guard ownership bug, swept out of the three functions where it remained. Running the advisor query against hosted after 127 found `sign_waiver` (129); this migration is the SWEEP that followed — every SECURITY DEFINER function checked for an `if not (...)` guard comparing a nullable column to `auth.uid()` with a bare `=`. **`cancel_booking`, `respond_to_offer` and `choose_pay_at_desk`** each read `if not (m.user_id = auth.uid() or is_desk_up(...)) then raise`, which does NOT fire when `m.user_id` is NULL (every unclaimed guest/lead/import), so any signed-in user could cancel, respond-to-offer-for, or pay-at-desk-for such a member's booking at any studio — the worst being `cancel_booking`, a core function live since migration 037. `coalesce(m.user_id = auth.uid(), false)` on all three. `join_challenge` was the one already written the safe way (`exists(select 1 ... where user_id = auth.uid())`, which returns false not null), and is now the documented pattern. `test/null_guard_test.sql` (UUID `40a1`) — an unclaimed member's booking, and a signed-in attacker refused PT403 by each of the three — plus three teeth checks (reverting each coalesce reopens its hole), and `scripts/check-null-guards.py` to catch the fifth before it ships.
- **129** two advisor findings on hosted after 127, both closed. **`sign_waiver()` let ANY authenticated member sign ANOTHER member's waiver** — for any member with a NULL `user_id`, which is every unclaimed guest, lead and import, at any studio. The guard read `m.user_id = auth.uid() or is_desk_up(...)`, and `null = uid` is NULL, so `not (NULL or false)` is NULL and `if NULL then raise` was SKIPPED — migration 020's lesson exactly, a boolean auth test that can be NULL is a hole in a SECURITY DEFINER function, and signing a waiver clears the §2.1 booking gate so this was a real cross-tenant WRITE. `coalesce(m.user_id = auth.uid(), false)`. Found by probing `sign_waiver` as a genuine member against another member's id — it returned NOT REFUSED. **`guest_pass_eligibility()` was granted to `authenticated` with no caller check** and returns whether an email is a member/guest of a given studio — tenant data, an email-enumeration surface across tenants. Nothing on the client calls it (`book_guest` calls it internally, and is SECURITY DEFINER so the internal call survives the revoke), so it LOSES THE GRANT rather than gaining a guard (086/103's answer for a pure internal). Teeth: reverting the coalesce reopens the cross-member write.
- **128** Decision 26, part two — chasing the guest waiver, and the paper fallback. An unsigned guest turned away at the door with nobody having chased them is the worst first impression of a business they were weighing joining. `sweep_guest_waivers()` (cron every **15 minutes**, a fixed **4-hour** lead) reminds the GUEST if their pass is still `invited` a few hours before the class and NUDGES THE HOST — the friend who invited them is who can make it happen — each once (dedupe on the pass). The guest's link is a fresh `/claim/<token>` if they have not set up an account (only the hash was ever stored, so a new token is minted and supersedes the old), or the app home if they have, where the waiver banner waits. **The paper fallback:** `record_document` re-issued so filing a guest's waiver (front desk, at the door) confirms the pass and clears the check-in gate exactly as signing in the app — a studio hands an unsigned guest a form rather than sending them home, and the product records it as a real filed document. `render_notification` and `notification_wanted` re-issued for the two new always-send templates (`guest_waiver_reminder` takes the footerless-invite treatment — an unclaimed guest has no settings screen). UI: a roster "Waiver signed on paper" button under an unsigned guest (`recordPaperWaiver`), gone once signed. Verified in the browser: the button recorded the paper waiver, the pass went `confirmed`, and a waiver document was filed. `sweep_guest_waivers` is service-role only. Assertions folded into `test/guest_pass_test.sql` (now 39) + two teeth checks (the sweep's lead window, and record_document confirming the pass).
- **127** Decision 26 — guest passes, MEMBERS bring a guest and the guest's first class is free. Optional per studio (`guest_passes_enabled`, off by default, no trace when off). `book_guest()` (host session) ensures the host holds a real seat, booking them if they had none, and gives the guest a second seat in the SAME occurrence — so a guest never takes a seat the host didn't also take, a full class needs TWO free seats (refused `only_one_seat` when one short), and held waitlist seats (§4.2) are respected. **Free once ever, keyed on email**: a unique index on `guest_passes(studio_id, lower(email))` plus a check against `members` (distinct sentences — `already_had_free` vs `already_member`). **One guest at a time**: a partial unique index while a pass is `invited`/`confirmed`; attending or cancelling frees the host. **The guest seat is free and separate** — `payment_source 'comp'`, no membership, no credit, no peak (the guest is a real member, status `'lead'`, Decision 15). `book_class` can't book the guest (the host is neither the guest's self nor desk), so `book_guest` inserts the comp seat directly under the occurrence lock and calls `book_class` only for the HOST's own seat. **Host cancels → guest STANDS and is told** (`tg_guest_pass_sync` queues `guest_host_cancelled`; the guest booking is untouched). **The waiver blocks CHECK-IN, not booking**: the guest is booked with an unsigned waiver, and `enforce_guest_waiver` (a trigger on `check_ins`, the single choke point for staff/QR/import/job) refuses check-in until they sign — `sign_waiver()` is self-serve in the app and confirms the pass; a guest unsigned at class time is turned away and the desk told. The guest is invited to claim (the 073 minter, replicated inline since `create_member_invite`/`invite_member` guard desk and the host is a member). **Conversion is derived** — a guest converted iff their member row holds any membership — surfaced by `guest_pass_report()` and `dashboard_guest_kpi` (null, and so absent, for a studio not running guests or with none yet). `render_notification` re-issued to give `guest_invite`/`guest_host_cancelled` the always-send list and the no-settings-link invite footer; `studio_member_settings()` re-issued (drop+recreate, RETURNS TABLE) to carry the flag to the member app. UI: a `/settings` toggle; a "Bring a guest — their first class is free" `<details>` on the class detail (member-driven, refusals as sentences, one-free-ever/one-at-a-time copy); a Home waiver banner for an unsigned guest; a roster flag ("Guest · first time" + "Brought by X"); the dashboard KPI. Verified in the browser: a member booked a guest (comp seat, lead, invite queued), the roster showed the flag, and the desk sees "this guest has not signed the waiver yet" on an unsigned check-in. 32 assertions in `test/guest_pass_test.sql` + three teeth checks (the waiver trigger, the two-seat need, the free-once guard). Instructor guests are NOT built.
- **126** the waitlist seat HOLD — §4.2, the gap migration 125 flagged and did not fix. `book_class`'s capacity gate read `booked_count`, which counts real seats only; a pending offer's booking stays `waitlisted`, so neither `booked_count` nor `occurrence_seats_taken` included it and general booking could take the seat from under a member who had been formally offered it. **Derived at gate time, never cached** (the guidance's preferred half): `occurrence_seats_held(occurrence, exclude_member)` counts live offers (`outcome is null and expires_at > now()`), `book_class` gates on `booked_count + held >= capacity`, and `booked_count` goes on counting real seats only — so `reconcile_booked_counts` stays a no-op and the reconcile test still agrees. **The hold ends the instant the offer does**: accepted, declined, expired or voided-at-the-cutoff all clear it, and an expired-but-unswept offer (the sweep runs every 5 minutes) holds nothing because of the `expires_at > now()` gate — a stale hold would be a permanently unbookable seat, worse than the bug. **The offered member's own offer is excluded**, or accepting through `respond_to_offer` (which cancels the waitlist row and re-enters `book_class` while the offer is still pending) would be refused for the very seat they were offered. **Front desk is not held**: the hold is folded into "full" and a desk override (§14, a deliberate act with a reason) books straight through it, recorded as `held_seat` rather than `capacity` since the class is not over capacity — the offer stands and fails on acceptance. The member `/book` screen subtracts the hold from the visible spaces and says WHY a class with a free seat is full — "Full · 1 on hold" — through `occurrence_holds()` (member-guarded, only occurrences with a hold come back, so nothing renders for a studio with none). Verified in the browser as a member: a held-full class reads "Full · 1 on hold" with Join waitlist while the bookable class beside it still says Book. 23 assertions in `test/waitlist_hold_test.sql` (a general booker waitlisted by the hold, the offered member accepting through it, the seat reopening the instant an offer expires / is declined / is voided at the cutoff, the desk override recorded `held_seat`, the reconcile finding nothing, two studios), and three teeth checks — removing the hold, the self-exclusion, or the `expires_at` gate each fails its matching assertion.
- **125** the waitlist sweep — §4.2's cascade and §4.4's cutoff, neither of which existed. **Diagnosed first:** only `cancel_booking` ever made an offer (one, to the front waiter, with the §4.3 clamp already applied inline); `respond_to_offer`'s decline closed the offer and stopped; an expired offer sat `outcome=null` forever and nobody was told; nineteen crons touched none of it, though the `(expires_at) where outcome is null` index was built for this on day one. `sweep_waitlist()` (cron every **5 minutes** — the actionable window between an offer expiring and the cutoff can be 15 minutes, so a 15-minute job could burn a third of it) does three ordered passes across all studios in each studio's own settings: **EXPIRE** a pending offer past its window and drop that member from the front (FIFO moves on, as a decline does); **CUTOFF** — inside `waitlist_cutoff_minutes`, close every remaining waitlisted entry with a new `waitlist_missed` "didn't get in" notice and void any pending offer; **PROMOTE** — a free seat with waiters, no pending offer, outside the cutoff, gets the next offer through the shared `offer_waitlist_seat()` (one definition of §4.2/§4.3, cross-referenced to `cancel_booking`'s inline copy). This is what cascades an expiry and a decline. Idempotent — closed rows are not re-selected and a pending offer blocks a second, so a re-run is a no-op. The seat-hold half of §4.2 ("held for the offered member") is **fixed in migration 126**: `book_class` gates on `booked_count` plus live offers, so a pending offer holds its seat.

- **124** a challenge cover photograph and a real progress trail. `challenges.cover_image_url` has existed since 001; `cover_image_focus_x/y` are added so a wide photo is not cropped to a sixth of itself on a phone (the 116 focal-point treatment). `member_challenges` and `member_challenge_detail` carry the cover + focus so the member list is photograph cards and the detail leads with a hero (accent-gradient fallback when there is none, like the member hero). Staff upload on the create form and re-anchor on the overview through the `studio-branding` bucket, the same path and policy as the class-type and login photos. And `member_challenge_detail.history` becomes a trail worth reading: every counted class WITH its instructor, and every class in the window that did NOT count with the reason — a late cancel or a no-show — so the number is trusted rather than argued with. Verified in the browser: the cover renders on the card and the hero at its focal point, and the trail reads "Barre · Saturday 5 September · Bo Fictitious · Counted" with the not-counting path confirmed in SQL ("Barre · you cancelled late").
- **122–123** challenges follow-up. **122** `studio_settings.challenges_enabled` — the DOOR. The rail item was gated on the studio already HAVING a challenge, which left no way to create the first one (`/challenges/new` reachable only by URL). Now the rail shows when the switch is on OR a challenge exists, and the switch is a toggle in `/settings` the way guarantees, seat caps, peak and publication are — off by default, so a studio that never turns it on still sees no trace. The switch governs the STAFF entry point only; member visibility stays gated on a published challenge existing, so turning it off never rug-pulls a member out of one they joined. **123** two fixes: a join deadline already in the PAST produced a challenge nobody could ever join (the constraint only checks the deadline falls within `[starts_on, ends_on]`, and a challenge that already started can have a past deadline), so `create_challenge`/`update_challenge` now reject `join_deadline < studio_today` and the create form defaults the deadline to a week out and warns; and `member_challenge_detail` gains `can_join` so the member app shows a running challenge past its deadline as **visible but closed** ("Started 8 September · joining closed") rather than hiding it — the deadline governs JOINING, not visibility, and a joined member always sees theirs regardless.

- **117–121** challenges, §9, **members only**. **117** adds `challenges.leaderboard_enabled` (Decision 10, off by default, per challenge) and `notification_preferences.challenge_email`, and closes two RLS holes live since 001: `cp_member_self` was `FOR ALL` — a member could POST their own `progress` to the goal or INSERT a participant row past the deadline — now `SELECT` only, with joining and every progress write behind SECURITY DEFINER; and `cp_leaderboard` now requires the challenge's board to be on (a member's OWN row still comes through `cp_member_self`, so board-off never hides a member's own progress), rewritten through `auth_participates_in()` because a policy on `challenge_participants` that queried `challenge_participants` recursed — the original never fired only because no member session had read it. **118** the engine: `challenge_qualifies()` (one predicate — occurrence in the studio-local date range, class-type filter), `recompute_participant()` (progress rebuilt purely from events per §9.4 — a count, or the CURRENT consecutive-week run for a streak, §9.5), `rank_challenge()` (§9.4 ordering), `join_challenge()` (deadline-guarded, backfills every qualifying attendance since the start, §9.2), and `tg_challenge_progress` on `bookings` — the synchronous §8 hook that catches a desk check-in AND a no-show corrected to attended, deletes a booking's events when it loses `attended`, and excludes imports (no booking) while comp counts. **119** five templates + `queue_challenge_*` wrappers + `sweep_challenges()` (studio-local status transitions and ending-soon, cron every 30m). **120** the app functions: `create_challenge`/`update_challenge`/`publish_challenge` (audience fixed to member), `staff_challenges`/`challenge_overview`, `member_challenges`/`member_challenge_detail` (the leaderboard returned only when the board is on). **121** brings back the Challenge Participation KPI as `dashboard_challenge_kpi()` (null — and so absent — for a studio with no published challenge) and drops the "coming soon" placeholder from `dashboard_absent_cards()`. **Optional per studio by EXISTENCE, not a switch** (Decisions 24/25's rule): a studio that never creates a challenge shows no member tab entry, no `/challenges` reachability, no KPI, no rail item — asserted with a no-challenge studio. The `challenge_opportunity` brief insight stays gated by its own `challenge_enabled` config (default 0), so a no-challenge studio sees no nudge either; its button now resolves to `/challenges/new`. **Instructor challenges are NOT built** — the `audience` column and instructor unique indexes stay unused; if they ever arrive they are AUTO-ENROLLED and system-awarded, not joined, which is a different mechanism.

- **001** schema: 47 tables, 110 RLS policies, grants for `authenticated` and `service_role`
- **002** `book_class()`: the booking transaction — occurrence locked `for update`, §2.1 eligibility gate in order with a specific reason code per failure, §2.2 payment source resolution, waitlist, booking + `credit_ledger` + `booked_count` in one transaction
- **003** `bookings.override_reason` / `overridden_rules` (§2.3 visibility), and `p_payment_source` so §2.4 comp bookings are reachable
- **004** `studio_by_slug()` — the pre-login lookup behind `{slug}.studiior.app`, and the only function `anon` may execute
- **005** comment-only: corrects migration 001 §16, which says there is no pre-login surface in V1. There is exactly one, and §16 also missed that PostgreSQL grants EXECUTE to `PUBLIC` by default
- **006** revokes `PUBLIC` execute on the auth and RLS helpers and changes the default for future functions, so §16's intent is actually true
- **007** the §8 check-in window as a trigger, with `studio_settings.checkin_window_enforced` as the documented way off
- **008** `plan_templates` (studio_id null = system) with six system templates, and a trigger refusing to delete a plan that has members on it
- **009** CHECK constraints making the plan-type field rules real (a pack cannot bill monthly, a subscription cannot expire), and one active plan per name per studio
- **010** Decision 12: `credits` is class-pack only, `credits_per_period` is recurring-only and null there means unlimited. The fifth constraint 009 left open
- **011** closes the anon RPC surface for real (006 revoked from `PUBLIC`, but hosted grants `anon` explicitly — see the rule below), makes trigger functions callable by nobody, and pins `search_path` on the five that predate the convention
- **012** onboarding: `platform_admins`, `studio_invites` (hashed, single-use), `studios.status = 'provisioning'`, `setup_progress`, and `accept_studio_invite()` — account, owner row and status flip in one transaction
- **013** revokes execute on `rls_auto_enable()`, the Supabase-installed event trigger function behind `ensure_rls`. Guarded, because it exists only on hosted — which is why 011 missed it
- **014** `revoke execute on all functions in schema public from anon`, then re-grants the three pre-login surfaces. Also moves `btree_gist` out of `public` — the only way its 188 index internals could leave the API-exposed schema, since `postgres` cannot revoke grants `supabase_admin` made
- **015** `validate_iana_timezone()` on `studios` and `locations` — a zone not in `pg_timezone_names` cannot be stored by any writer. Plus ISO shape checks on currency and country, and `provision_studio()` fixed forward with `invalid_timezone` / `invalid_currency` / `invalid_country`
- **016** the importer's schema half: nullable `check_ins.occurrence_id` / `booking_id` so an imported visit can exist without a class, an `import_id` marker, `check_ins_booked_or_imported` (a check-in is either an import or has both a booking and an occurrence), and `recompute_member_stats()`
- **017** demo data: `is_demo` on twelve tables, `generate_demo_data()` and `purge_demo_data()`, platform-admin only. Every id is derived from the studio id, so two runs produce identical data
- **018** Decision 14 health score: `member_health()` (pure), the cache on `members`, `refresh_studio_health()` for the nightly pass, and a trigger recomputing on check-in. Includes the `new` band for members joined under 14 days, per the amendment recorded in Decision 14
- **019** the importer's function half: `import_dry_run()`, `import_commit()`, `import_rollback()`. Also `import_member_status()` / `import_membership_status()`, which both halves share — a file saying "Active" against a lowercase enum must fail at review, not inside the commit transaction the review just promised was safe
- **021** the member journey timeline: `rebuild_member_timeline()` / `rebuild_studio_timeline()`. Data model §4 asks for one writer that is testable and replayable, so every event is *derived* from its source and the whole thing can be dropped and rebuilt without drifting. `booked` is deliberately not emitted — it tells every attended class twice and every cancelled one twice
- **116** flex cancellation, by audience. `flex_reached_minimum_at` — the latch moved EARLY: crossing the minimum stamps it and tells the instructor once ("going ahead"), the formal commit still happens at the cutoff so `booked_at_cutoff` stays the real count and per-head pay is untouched, and `evaluate_commitment()` now commits a class that EVER reached its minimum even if a member has since dropped below. `schedule_range()` dropped/recreated to carry `occ_cancellation_cause` and to keep unmet-minimum rows on the calendar (a generic cancellation stays hidden), so a not-running slot is visible to staff with the count that caused it. The instructor's deadline digest (`sweep_commitments`) and member invisibility (RLS + a `/book` status filter) already covered the other two audiences. The threshold trigger calls the UNGUARDED `occurrence_guarantee_run()` — it runs as the booking member, and the guarded name would refuse them (056's pattern)
- **115** reliability, MEASURED and never enforced. `shift_applications` gains `approved_at` (set once, never cleared — a lifetime "approved for" count), `withdrawn_at` and `withdrawal_notice_hours` (snapshot). `withdraw_application()` restores withdrawing a PENDING application — a live regression since 064, where the portal called `withdraw_from_shift()` which requires being the assigned instructor and 403'd on an open shift. `withdraw_from_shift()` (the approved-backout→cover path) additionally records the withdrawal. `instructor_reliability()` returns applied/approved/withdrawn and the notice on each, short-notice measured against the studio's own `cover_escalation_hours`. Nothing reads back into who is offered a shift
- **114** a staff control to OPEN a shift, and an alert to who could take it. `open_shift()` takes an instructor off a class (through `move_occurrence()`, so it hits the same checks and the `assigned_by` stamp), keeps it bookable, TELLS the instructor removed (or reports them uncontactable), and audits with who and why. `tg_stamp_shift_opened` stamps `shift_opened_at` on every transition to an open shift; `notify_open_shifts()` (cron, 15 min) emails every QUALIFIED instructor, batched into one digest, available-and-in-window first with the rest marked "outside the hours you gave us", no-login ones reported uncontactable
- **113** Decision 25's second half: `my_month_roster()`, `confirm_month_roster()` — one action for the month, with `request_cover()` as the flag — and the brief learning `month_unpublished` (ranked 0, above an unstaffed class, because it is every class at once) and `month_roster_unconfirmed`, plus two action-centre rows. Also counts migration 074's `studio_closed_with_bookings` in `brief_summary()`, which had never been added there: a brief whose only item was a class on a closed day opened with "Nothing needs you this morning" above it
- **112** Decision 25: a month is a DRAFT until the studio publishes it. `publication_enabled` (default false), `schedule_publications` per studio per month with no writer for removal, `month_published()` as the one predicate — true when the switch is off and for any month before the current one — read by the two RLS policies, `book_class()` rule 2.1.1b, every instructor reader, the flex sweep and `queue_instructor_assigned()`. `publish_month()` emails each instructor their own classes IN the body and names anybody with no login; `set_publication_enabled()` auto-publishes the current month and any month members have booked into. **Also moves `move_occurrence()`'s availability-dates refusal ahead of its UPDATE** — it had returned ok:false with the row already reassigned since 061
- **111** a studio says what matters in its photograph, so the crop keeps it. `login_image_focus_x/y` on `studios` and `image_focus_x/y` on `class_types`, defaulting to 50/50 — which is exactly what `object-fit: cover` already does, so nothing renders differently until somebody moves the point. `studio_by_slug()` carries it, which meant dropping and recreating one of the **nine pre-login surfaces**
- **110** `schedule_range()` returns the tier a class IS beside the tier it will BEHAVE as, plus the minimum that decides whether a flex one runs. `occurrence_guarantee()` demotes a tier whose switch is off to 'always' — correctly — but that is studio-wide, so a calendar drawn from it marked every core class 'always' and disagreed with the series list beside it. A `RETURNS TABLE` cannot gain a column through `create or replace`, so it drops first and re-asserts its ACL
- **109** the numbers a studio decides with, and the one message worth sending: `peak_allowance_report()` states BOTH readings of its own exhaustion figure rather than leaving an owner to guess which end they are at, and `sweep_peak_cutoff_reminders()` tells a member holding a peak class that the free window is closing — the only message in this product that changes what happens rather than reporting what already has
- **108** Decision 24's third switch: `member_infractions`, and a suspension that is **DERIVED, never stored**. Also `sweep_no_shows()`, because nothing in this product has ever written a no-show
- **107** `member_peak_slots()` — what the member app asks about peak, in one question over the range the page is already fetching. Per class: is it peak, how many peak classes are left in THAT CLASS'S period, and when the period ends. Nothing on the client works any of it out: peak-ness is a half-open comparison against a table of wall times and a period is a fixed week from `week_starts_on`, and a TypeScript copy of either would agree until the first studio whose week starts on Sunday
- **106** Decision 24: the peak allowance itself. `peak_allowance_ledger` shaped like `credit_ledger` — a REVERSING ROW keyed on the booking, never a decrement, with a unique index on `(booking_id, reason)` so any writer may fire twice; `peak_allowance_state()` as the one definition of how much is left; `tg_peak_allowance()` as an AFTER trigger on `bookings`, because a booking's status is changed from more places than anybody will remember; and one new rule in `book_class()`, placed **after** §2.2 so the plan that PAYS is the plan held to its allowance. **There is no migration 105** — see below
- **104** Decision 24: when peak is, and which plans are held to it. `peak_windows` (per studio, per day of week, as studio-local WALL TIMES so a clock change moves nothing), `peak_allowance_enabled`, `peak_allowance` / `peak_allowance_period` per plan, and `occurrence_is_peak()` as the one predicate — half-open, so 16:55 against a 17:00 window is off-peak and so is a 19:00 class against a window that ends at 19:00. **Schema and one predicate: nothing here changes what happens when anybody books.** `daily_booking_cap` is deliberately absent — it already exists as `max_bookings_per_day` and `book_class()` rule 2.1.7 has enforced it since migration 002
- **103** `membership_frozen_now()` loses a grant it never needed. Found by running the advisor query against HOSTED after 102 and then asking migration 056's half of it: the function is SECURITY DEFINER, takes a membership id, steps over the RLS on `memberships` and answers a boolean about it, and 095 granted it to `authenticated` with nothing asking for one. **The grant goes rather than a guard arriving** — 086's stronger answer — because its only callers are three functions that have already checked
- **102** Decision 24 part one: a plan may hold only so many people. `seat_caps_enabled` per studio (default false), `max_active_members` / `show_remaining_below` / `on_limit_reached` per plan, `plan_seats_taken()` as the one definition of a taken place and `plan_seats()` as what a screen asks. The place is claimed inside `activate_purchase()` **under a row lock on the plan**, because counting and then inserting is the check-then-act that overbooks — and a completed Stripe checkout is deliberately exempt, since the money has already moved
- **097** instructors can sign in, and there is something for them to open: `invite_instructor()`, `claim_instructor_account()`, `my_instructor()`, and `instructor_roster()` — the §14 boundary written into the reader so no screen can forget it
- **096** backfills the periods migration 095 only fixed going forward. A fix that applies only to rows created after it is half a fix
- **095** Decision 23: a recurring membership gets a PERIOD at activation, a cash payment against an existing one advances it rather than selling a second, a lapsed period becomes `past_due` nightly, and `/due` finally answers who owes the studio money
- **094** `staff_bootstrap()` carries `studio_week_starts_on`, because the calendar has to know which day a week starts on BEFORE it can ask which seven days to fetch. A `returns table` cannot gain a column through `create or replace`, so this drops first — and a drop discards the ACL, which the migration revokes and then asserts
- **093** three things the dashboard got wrong on REAL data, fixed forward because 091 and 092 are applied on hosted: the activity feed read a payment's status as though it were the thing bought, the month's empty state offered to create a timetable to a studio that has one starting later, and the page title appeared twice
- **092** the AI layer, and the boundary drawn mechanically rather than promised: `dashboard_facts()` publishes the exact set of numbers a narrative may contain, `narrative_offending_number()` refuses any answer containing one outside it, and the deterministic sentence is written BEFORE the call so a refusal, a timeout, a missing key or an outage degrades to prose that is already correct
- **091** Bible Ch. 4's dashboard, every figure computed in SQL: `dashboard_kpis`, `dashboard_revenue`, `dashboard_heatmap`, `dashboard_health`, `dashboard_activity`, `dashboard_tasks`, `dashboard_month`, `dashboard_absent_cards`, and `studio_day_bounds()` as the range form of `studio_today()`. Every block distinguishes "this has never happened" from "it did not happen today"
- **090** `move_occurrence()` refused instructors who WERE available — `v_tz` was null on the ordinary path, so the date it checked was null — and its Decision 9 warning raised instead of warning
- **089** `create_occurrence()`: creating a class goes through the same gate as moving one. `createClass` was a bare INSERT with no validity window, no availability check, and a clash surfacing as a raw Postgres error
- **088** `set_series_guarantee()` had raised on every call since 081, which renamed `flex_confirmed_at` and missed it. Found by building the tier control — the writer had never been called by anything
- **087** `update_series()` reported its PREDICTION as its RESULT — `v_moves` was incremented only in the preview loop and returned as `moved`, so an edit that moved nothing still answered "19 moved". Now returns what the apply loop did, asks the calendar afterwards, and writes an audit row
- **086** eight SECURITY DEFINER functions from 080–083 had no guard inside them, found by running the advisor query against hosted after 085 landed and then asking the other half of the question. Also closes `set_series_flex` and `set_occurrence_guaranteed` to `anon`, which hosted had granted and local never showed
- **085** the calendar carries the tier and flags a standalone flex slot, resolved inside `schedule_range()` rather than fetched per block
- **084** the period statement per instructor, and `guarantee_report()` — the not_running rate by slot leads, because it is the number a studio can act on
- **083** the conversion bonus: attributed to the instructor of the member's FIRST EVER class, one per member ever, fired from the membership insert so cash and Stripe behave identically, clawed back as an adjustment
- **082** Decision 22's pay half, overturning part of Decision 10: `instructor_rate_versions` (immutable, effective-dated), `pay_periods`, `instructor_pay_records`, `compute_class_pay()` and a trigger writing it ONCE at the terminal transition
- **081** cutoff evaluation: `flex_confirmed_at` becomes `committed_at`, `booked_at_cutoff` snapshots the headcount, `commitment_pending()` and `sweep_commitments()` replace the flex-only pair, batched digests, and `force_commit_occurrence()`
- **080** guarantee tiers — `core` | `flex` | `always` on the series and per occurrence, both cutoff shapes kept apart, and `occurrence_is_adjacent()`
- **079** why a class was cancelled and why a booking was released, as typed data. `cancellation_reason` had existed since migration 001 with **nothing ever writing it** — 61 cancelled occurrences, 0 with a reason
- **078** archive, end and delete for a series, and the cascade behind delete closed: `series_impact()`, `archive_series()` two-step, `restore_series()`, `end_series()` (which goes through `update_series()`), `guard_series_delete()` and `guard_series_archive_path()`
- **077** `series_status` gains `'archived'`, alone, because a new enum value cannot be used in the transaction that adds it
- **076** converging hosted with the files, **a third time**: `generate_occurrences`, `schedule_range`, `flex_pending`, `flex_report` and `sweep_flex_decisions` re-issued from 075's file, plus the `studiior-flex-decisions` cron entry, which had never been scheduled on hosted either. Found by `scripts/check-hosted-drift.sh` on the first run after it was fixed — see the rule below. A structural diff beside it (columns, indexes, policies, triggers, constraints, cron jobs) found **nothing else missing**: 074 and 075's schema is entirely present on hosted and only their function bodies and that one cron entry had drifted
- **075** Decision 21, flex classes: `flex` and `minimum_bookings` on series and occurrences, a per-studio deadline in two shapes, `sweep_flex_decisions()` every fifteen minutes, `flex_pending()`, `flex_report()`, and `flex_confirmed_at` as a latch
- **074** studio closures: `studio_closures`, `studio_closed_at()` as the one predicate, `cancel_occurrence()` — the first caller `queue_occurrence_cancelled()` has ever had — `close_studio()` two-step, `reopen_studio()` which is not an undo, and a brief insight for a class sitting on a day the studio is shut
- **073** the member invite is finally SENT: `invite_member()`, `invite_members_bulk()` and `member_invite_status()`, a `member_invite` template that does not claim there is an app to download, and the two always-send lists learning it
- **072** `next_class_day()` — an empty calendar can finally say where the timetable actually is, plus a date picker, after "the calendar is empty" turned out to be a correct empty day nobody could navigate off
- **071** converging hosted with the files after **two migrations were edited in place once already applied** — `schedule_range` was raising on every call in production, and `purge_demo_data` had never gained its confirmation step there
- **070** `schedule_range()` and `studio_today()` — the calendar asks for a range of the STUDIO's days and the day boundary is resolved in the database, after it rendered an empty grid for every day
- **069** `reconcile_booked_counts()` — the nightly recount `booked_count` was assumed to already have and never had, plus `occurrence_seats_taken()` as the one definition of what the cache is a cache of
- **068** the occurrence horizon becomes days, defaults to 60, gains a screen, and finally does something when it SHORTENS: `set_occurrence_horizon()` two-step, deleting rather than cancelling and refusing outright over a booking
- **067** weekly confirmation: one press for the whole week, per-class cover beside it, and an ask/remind/escalate cycle whose every day and window is a per-studio column
- **066** an instructor submits their own month and staff approve it: `availability_submissions`, `approval_status` defaulting to approved so nothing existing is invalidated, and the monthly cycle keyed on `availability_due_day`
- **065** the commitment stops scheduling anybody: assignment ranks by fewest classes that week and nothing else, the availability window stops being defaulted from the agreement, and `commitment_report()` gives the measurement the table exists for
- **064** `class_series` finally has a form, and editing one stops being destructive: `update_series()` two-step, `rrule_last_date()` folding COUNT into a date, `series_rule_matches()`, `generate_occurrences()` gaining a `p_from`, and the setup checklist learning qualifications, availability and commitments
- **063** a demo row a human edits becomes real, and the purge asks first: `tg_promote_edited_demo_row()` on the seven tables with an edit form, and `purge_demo_data(studio_id, confirm)` refusing until confirmed
- **062** `purge_demo_data` destroyed real data on production. The function only ever deleted `is_demo` rows; `class_occurrences.series_id -> class_series` is ON DELETE CASCADE, so deleting a demo series took every occurrence of it whatever the occurrence's own flag said. Detaches real children from demo parents first, marks what the generator caused, and **counts every non-demo row before and after, raising if one has gone**
- **061** the assignment engine: `assign_instructors_run()` behind the manager-up `assign_instructors()`, `instructor_valid_on()`, `class_occurrences.assigned_by`, and the validity gate added to `move_occurrence()`
- **060** `instructor_class_types` — who can teach what, written from either side, with `instructor_qualified()`
- **059** the member screen's three read-only sections get writers, and documents get built: `rebuild_timeline_rows()` (the derivation, unguarded) behind the guarded `rebuild_member_timeline()`, triggers on the four source tables, `backfill_all_timelines()`, `member_goal_progress()`, `member_documents` + a private bucket, and `record_document()` which sets `members.waiver_signed_at`
- **058** archive and delete for class types, rooms and instructors: `archive_impact()`, `archive_record()` two-step, `restore_record()`, three delete guards, and a trigger making `archive_record()` the only way to reach `status = 'archived'`
- **057** data model §5's occurrence generation, specified since the beginning and never built: `generate_occurrences(series_id)`, a nightly `generate_all_occurrences()` claiming per studio in `job_runs`, and materialise-on-create/edit as a trigger. Adds `class_occurrences.series_slot_at` and `studio_settings.occurrence_horizon_months`, and makes a move set `is_exception`, which nothing had ever done
- **056** guards three `SECURITY DEFINER` reads that had none: `instructor_availability_week` and `instructor_weekly_load` from 053, and `instructor_available_at` — which has carried the fault since **047**. Manager-up of that instructor's studio, or the instructor themselves
- **055** the brief learns two Decision 18 types: `cover_unanswered`, ranked **0** inside the escalation window — above `unstaffed_class`, which 17 already put above a declined card — and `commitment_shortfall`, the reason `instructor_commitments` exists at all
- **054** cover requests: `request_cover`, `approve_cover_request` (assign a named replacement, or open it as a Decision 17 shift), `decline`, `withdraw`, the five-minute escalation sweep, five templates, and the first caller `queue_substitution()` has ever had. Also scopes `send_due_notifications()` to `channel = 'email'` — it claimed every scheduled row and posted it to Resend, so the first push row anyone queued would have been delivered as an email
- **053** Decision 18's schema: `instructor_commitments`, `set_instructor_availability()` (a whole week, replaced in one call), `set_availability_exception()`, `instructor_availability_week()` and `instructor_weekly_load()`
- **052** `staff_bootstrap()` — the same collapse for the staff app: the staff row, the studio, its primary location, onboarding state, the platform-admin flag and billing state in one request instead of five in sequence
- **051** `member_bootstrap(slug)` — the member context, the studio, its member-facing settings, its billing state and any live offer in ONE request. Replaced four that had to run in sequence
- **050** what a move costs: the instructor constraint becomes DEFERRABLE so a swap is possible, a significant move grants every booked member a free cancellation (finally implementing `sub_late_free_cancel`, declared in 001 and read by nothing), an undo window withdraws the unsent email, and a blocked move names the class in the way
- **049** the Morning Brief learns `unstaffed_class` — an open shift with members booked, ranked above a failed card because a declined card can wait until Thursday and a 7am class cannot. Also fixes a latent `text[] || text || text` bug in `brief_summary()` that appended an empty part
- **048** applications: `apply_for_shift`, `approve_shift_application` (auto-declines the rest in the same transaction), `decline`, `withdraw_from_shift`, five templates, and `class_moved` for members whose class is rescheduled
- **047** Decision 17's schema — `staffing_state`, `shift_applications`, the first GiST exclusion constraints in the codebase, and `move_occurrence()`, the only thing that moves a class
- **046** the platform webhook — a SECOND endpoint with a SECOND secret, refusing anything carrying an `account` — plus the grace warnings, `sweep_platform_billing()` on a daily cron, and `render_notification()` extended to address a person at the studio rather than only a member
- **045** what a locked studio cannot do: `book_class`, `resolve_checkin_code`, `import_commit` and `record_manual_payment`. Deliberately NOT `cancel_booking`, and not any read
- **044** `platform_subscriptions` — Studiior charging the studio, $79/month USD, thirty-day trial stamped by a trigger on `studios` as well as by `provision_studio()`, plus `studio_is_locked()`, `studio_billing_state()` and `extend_trial()`
- **043** `choose_pay_at_desk()` — a member at a studio that HAS a provider can still say they will pay at the counter. Confirms the held seat and leaves a pending payment for the desk
- **042** `studio_setup_state()` gains an `optional` flag per item, so a checklist can say "nice to have" rather than nagging forever
- **041** Stripe stops being the foundation and becomes the first adapter: its handlers call `activate_purchase()` and `confirm_dropin_payment()` instead of granting anything themselves, and every row it writes is stamped `provider = 'stripe'`
- **040** Decision 16: `payments.provider`, `method`, `reference`, `recorded_by`; `record_manual_payment()` for front desk and up; `record_refund()` for managers and up; and the two shared grant functions both providers go through
- **039** the setup checklist stops taking a studio's word for Stripe: `connect_stripe` derived from `stripe_account_id` rather than from the stub's stored flag, which was the one item in that checklist that could go stale
- **038** Stripe Connect Standard: OAuth with a single-use state, hosted Checkout on the connected account, `stripe_webhook()` and six handlers. §7.3's `invoice.payment_failed` finally calls `queue_payment_failed()`, which migration 031 left with no caller
- **037** a drop-in holds its seat while the member pays — `book_class()` and `cancel_booking()` replaced from their live definitions, plus `sweep_unpaid_dropins()` on pg_cron
- **036** `booking_status = 'pending_payment'` and `studio_settings.dropin_payment_window_minutes`. Its own migration because a new enum value cannot be USED in the transaction that adds it
- **035** a member's own profile — `preferred_name`, a private `member-avatars` bucket whose write policy keys on the member's own id, `class_types.image_url`, a class-type image policy for managers (029's is owner-only, and Permissions §4 gives class types to managers too), and `guard_member_self_update()`. That trigger is the point: `members_self_update` had no column restriction, so a member could sign her own waiver and promote herself past the §2.1 gate
- **034** the four faults in a real delivered booking confirmation: a reply-to (`studios.contact_email`, new and nullable — absent means no header rather than a fake one), the room folded into the sentence instead of stranded in its own paragraph, a footer of contact details and an email-settings link instead of the studio name repeated under a rule, and an accent that falls back to neutral grey rather than to Studiior's lime
- **033** closes the notification functions. Migration 030 revoked them from `PUBLIC`, which on hosted leaves the `authenticated` grant untouched — so any signed-in member of any studio could call `notification_api_key()` and read the Resend key, `send_via_resend()` to send arbitrary mail from our domain, or `render_notification()` to read another tenant's email in cleartext. Also closes 031's three trigger functions, which came out **anon**-callable
- **031** wires §12's events with triggers rather than call sites — a booking is made from four different places and a notification that depends on each caller remembering is one the next caller will miss. Also skips demo members, and an hourly credit-expiry sweep because "seven days before" is a date in the studio's timezone, not an event
- **032** `create extension if not exists pg_net`, which had been enabled by hand on hosted and appeared in no migration — a fresh project applied all thirty-one and then failed a minute later, inside cron, with `schema "net" does not exist`. Also narrows two search_paths back to `public`
- **030** notification delivery, email only: `queue_notification()` checks preferences at queue time, `send_due_notifications()` claims with `for update skip locked` and posts through a transport behind a config row, `reconcile_notification_sends()` turns the provider's answer into sent or failed. pg_cron every minute for both
- **029** studio branding for the member app: `theme_preset` (warm/clean/calm/bold), a shape-checked `accent_color`, a `studio-branding` storage bucket whose write policy keys on the studio id in the first path segment, and `studio_by_slug()` fixed forward to carry the branding — the login screen and the tab title are both branded before anyone signs in
- **028** Decision 15: §2.1 rule 5 passes `lead` as well as `active`, and a second guard after §2.2 refuses a lead resolving to anything but `drop_in`. Replaced `book_class()` forward **from the live definition**, not from 002's text — 003 added a fifth parameter, so re-issuing 002's four-argument signature creates a second overload and every call fails as ambiguous
- **027** member accounts: `member_invites` (hashed, single-use, one live per member), `claim_member_account()` for the invite path, and `claim_member_by_email()` for self-signup — which refuses until `auth.users.email_confirmed_at` is set
- **025** the member app's missing half: `occ_member_own_read` (a member may read a class at any status if they have a booking or check-in for it), `rooms_member_read`, `studio_member_settings()`, the rotating check-in code, `cancel_booking()` per §3.1, and `respond_to_offer()`. Written after asking a real member session what it returns, not after designing screens
- **024** the clock behind the brief: `is_service_context()`, `run_due_morning_briefs()`, and a pg_cron job every 15 minutes. `studios_due_for_brief()` had existed since 023 with nothing calling it, so no brief ever generated on its own
- **023** the Morning Brief: `insight_config` (every §11 threshold as a row a studio can move), `generate_morning_brief()`, `brief_summary()`, `studios_due_for_brief()` and `set_insight_status()`. Writes `ai_insights` and `morning_briefs`, which had existed since 001 with nothing writing them. No model is called and `model` / `prompt_version` stay null — a row naming a model it never saw would be worse than an empty column
- **022** `messages` and `message_templates`: one person writing to one member, per Permissions §12 — owner, manager and front desk, never instructors. Nothing sends. `send_message()` moves a draft to `queued` and stops, so a transport becomes one adapter reading queued rows rather than a refactor. `message_draft_for()` composes from the band's reason, one draft per reason, out of a table a studio can later edit
- **020** `is_manager_up()` and `is_desk_up()` return false rather than null for a caller who is staff of no studio. `auth_role_in()` gives null, `null in (...)` is null, and every guard in the codebase is written `if not is_manager_up(x) then raise` — which does nothing against a null. Harmless in the ~110 policies that use these (a policy denies on null); a hole in every SECURITY DEFINER function that used them as a gate. See the rule below

**THE `all_off` CANARY IS THE THIRD STUDIO NOW.** The standard runs were two studios configured different ways; the third is a studio that turned NOTHING on — `insert into studio_settings (studio_id)` and nothing else, so every opt-in flag takes its default — carrying a real substrate (instructors with no logins, a timetable with a ran/cancelled/ahead class, members with bookings, a silent roster past its deadline). Every feature sweep is run against it and the whole opt-in surface must be inert: 0 pay records / periods / settlements, 0 committed occurrences, 0 peak ledger, 0 infractions, the absent member NOT marked no-show (suspension off gates `sweep_no_shows`), 0 challenges / guests / publications, 0 roster carried and not even DUE a carry, 0 notifications queued by the sweeps, `studio_uses_payroll` false and the guest/challenge KPIs null (absent, not zero). Teeth: turn guarantees on + put a rate on file and the identical cancellation writes a record — so every zero was the switch being off, not an empty studio. `test/all_off_test.sql` (UUID `0ff0`). **This is the assertion that catches the next default-that-does-something; extend it, do not retrofit a third studio into every suite.**

**THE DEFAULTS SWEEP (studio_settings, membership_plans, class_series, instructor_rate_versions), and the two defaults that DO something to a studio that opted into nothing.** Every opt-in flag defaults false (guarantees / flex / peak / seat_caps / suspension / challenges / guest_passes / publication / carry_forward / conversion_bonus) and every numeric gated behind one is inert while it is off — the canary proves it. The two previously-found leaks are NEUTRALISED and confirmed so: `core_unmet_pay_pct=50` is now gated (139), and `record_class_pay` on cancel is gated on `guarantees_enabled OR flex_enabled` (139) — the canary writes 0. `class_series.guarantee_tier` defaults `'core'` but `occurrence_guarantee()` demotes it to `'always'` behaviour while guarantees is off, so it is inert (110). `instructor_rate_versions` are all-zero and only exist if created. `membership_plans.freeze_allowed=true` is core membership, not an opt-in. **What WAS still the first kind — three instructor-obligation emails — is CLOSED in migration 142, each gated off by default:** (1) **weekly confirmation** was `week_confirm_enabled` DEFAULT true → flipped to false (existing rows kept); (2) **availability reminders** had no switch → new `availability_reminders_enabled` default false gates the sweep; (3) **pay check-in** (`sweep_instructor_confirmations`) gated explicitly on `guarantees_enabled OR flex_enabled`. The `all_off` canary now holds them with a login instructor + an in-window class on the ask day. Also recorded: **`job_runs` is a GLOBAL per-day claim** (`unique (job_key, run_for)`), so "the cron says so in job_runs for studio X" is not a thing the schema can express — a sweep writes at most one row for the whole run (`sweep_no_shows` writes `('no_shows', today, 'done')`), and several feature sweeps (`sweep_roster_carry`, `sweep_peak_cutoff_reminders`, `sweep_week_confirmations`) write no `job_runs` row at all. A per-studio "ran and did nothing" trace would be a new design, not a fix.

Sixty-two suites, all passing — run them with **`scripts/run-suites.sh`** (one `db reset` per suite; the older single-reset loop let cross-suite leftovers flip the anon-surface and global-count assertions, and once raced its own reset):

| Suite | Asserts | Covers |
|---|---|---|
| `test/rls_test.sql` | 36 | tenant isolation, role boundaries, restricted views |
| `test/book_class_test.sql` | 69 | authorisation, gate reason codes, payment resolution, overrides, comp, and migration 069's reconcile: a no-show keeps their seat, a hand-written booking drifts the cache and the recount fixes it, a dry run writes nothing, and a studio that only ever used book_class has no drift at all |
| `test/booking_concurrency_test.sql` | 20 | 50 simultaneous bookings against a 10-seat class |
| `test/checkin_window_test.sql` | 11 | §8 check-in window bounds, the settings that move them, the escape hatch |
| `test/plan_management_test.sql` | 56 | Permissions §9 on plans and templates, the delete guard, price snapshotting |
| `test/onboarding_test.sql` | 71 | platform-admin boundary, invite single-use and expiry, atomic acceptance, derived checklist, the stranded-user guards |
| `test/health_score_test.sql` | 59 | Decision 14's five signals in priority order, every band including `new` and `insufficient_history`, reasons carrying real numbers |
| `test/notifications_test.sql` | 55 | preferences suppress at queue time, a duplicate dedupe key is refused, a missing API key fails the row and not the cron, a second worker run cannot re-send a claimed one, no internal is executable by `authenticated` or `anon`, and a rendered email carries a reply-to, a folded room and a neutral accent |
| `test/member_accounts_test.sql` | 65 | an unverified email cannot claim an existing member, a used or expired token fails, one login holds two memberships without either seeing the other, a lead books drop-in only; and migration 073's invites — inviting queues exactly one notification, a resend sends a second email and kills the first link, a claimed invite cannot be reused, the mail carries the studio's name and accent and never Studiior's lime, and a member with a blank email is refused by name rather than failing silently |
| `test/member_app_test.sql` | 32 | history joins to real classes while someone else's past class stays hidden, the code rotates and only the desk resolves it, cancelling returns or consumes the credit and always frees the seat |
| `test/brief_schedule_test.sql` | 32 | a studio past its send time is picked up and one that is not is skipped, a second run the same day is a no-op, a half-finished run retries, and an authenticated caller with no JWT is still refused |
| `test/brief_test.sql` | 25 | the cap holds at five when twelve qualify, a dismissed subject stays gone seven days and comes back on the eighth, every `action_payload` href matches a route the app serves, retention_risk agrees with the band |
| `test/messages_test.sql` | 34 | one draft per reason, sending queues and never sends, the journey learns once, §12 including an instructor and a stranger |
| `test/timeline_test.sql` | 20 | derivation matches source, rebuilding twice does not double, a stranger and a front desk are both refused |
| `test/scheduling_test.sql` | 76 | a room cannot hold two classes and an instructor cannot teach two, a cancelled class stops holding its room, a move with members booked refuses until confirmed and then emails them, two instructors apply and approving one auto-declines the other, and withdrawing returns the class to open |
| `test/platform_billing_test.sql` | 39 | a studio in grace still books and takes money, past grace both apps lock, cancellation survives lockout, paying reinstates with row counts proving nothing was lost, and neither webhook endpoint accepts the other's events |
| `test/manual_payments_test.sql` | 80 | a studio with no provider sells a membership, grants a pack and takes a drop-in; a cash membership and a Stripe membership are the same row; a refund takes back the credits she had left and not the ones she used; front desk sells but cannot refund; and migration 095's periods — a cash recurring membership ends one month, one fortnight or one quarter out according to its own plan, a second payment advances the period from where the old one ended and leaves ONE membership, a payment against months of arrears says it is still owing, a lapsed period sweeps to past_due while a frozen one and a Stripe-backed one are left alone, and the due list shows the lapsed member and not the frozen one, the paid-up one or the class pack; and migration 103's — a stranger reads none of this studio's memberships directly AND cannot ask whether one is frozen, with the direct read asserted at 0 first so the refusal is known to be the function closing rather than the caller having nothing to see |
| `test/stripe_connect_test.sql` | 38 | a forged signature is refused, an event for an unknown account is rejected rather than misattributed, a replay is a no-op, a plan price rise does not reprice existing members, a failed payment blocks new bookings while existing ones stand, and an abandoned hold is swept back to the waitlist |
| `test/cover_and_commitment_test.sql` | 80 | a week entered in one go including copy-to-days, a re-entered week replacing rather than appending, an exception surviving it, an instructor who cannot lower their own minimum, a cover request leaving the class assigned, a same-day request escalating on arrival and the sweep catching one that became urgent later, approving into an open shift landing in Decision 17's application flow, Decision 2's free cancellation on a late substitution, and a replacement with no login being reported as unreachable |
| `test/occurrence_generation_test.sql` | 95 | a series materialises on create and a second run creates nothing, an occurrence moved away from its series is not regenerated into the slot it vacated, a 07:00 class stays 07:00 across the October DST change, two studios generate in one cron run and a second run the same day claims nothing, a clashing week is reported while the other fifty-one are still created, a rule the parser does not understand cannot be saved, shortening the horizon deletes rather than cancels so lengthening it refills the calendar completely, a booking beyond the new edge refuses the whole change and names it, and a moved class, a hand-assigned one and a one-off are all kept; and the calendar's range in two timezones in one run — a 07:00 Manila class stored at 23:00 UTC the previous day is on Manila's 18th and not its 17th, Prague's same date returns Prague's class and none of Manila's; and migration 074's closures in those same two zones in one run — the generator skips a closed period and says it did, closing cancels what was already there, a partial day takes only the classes inside its hours, one studio's closure is not the other's, and reopening regenerates what was never made while leaving cancelled classes cancelled |
| `test/demo_purge_test.sql` | 36 | a real class materialised against a demo series survives the series being purged and keeps its booking, real instructors, availability, commitments, members, class types, rooms, plans and series all survive, demo data regenerates cleanly afterwards, and a second round changes nothing |
| `test/assignment_test.sql` | 41 | a November-only instructor is not a December candidate and a dated exception removes one day inside the window, a qualified-but-unavailable instructor is left open rather than assigned, six classes split three and three by fewest that week, a target of 12 wins nobody an extra class and a target of 2 costs nobody one, an expired agreement does not make somebody unschedulable, a manual assignment survives a re-run, a dry run writes nothing, and the report measures against the minimum while somebody outside the studio cannot read it at all |
| `test/qualifications_test.sql` | 13 | an unmapped instructor is qualified for nothing, re-saving replaces rather than appends, another studio's class type cannot be mapped in, and an instructor reads their own and cannot decide it |
| `test/member_records_test.sql` | 36 | a booking or check-in writes the timeline as it happens and rebuilding does not double it, the backfill covers every studio, a goal counts only visits since it was set, a waiver upload signs the member so the booking gate agrees, an instructor sees no documents at all and front desk sees everything except the medical one, and another studio's owner sees none of it |
| `test/archive_test.sql` | 56 | an archived class type, room and instructor are invisible to a member and visible to staff, deleting a referenced record is refused and names what is in the way, archiving an instructor opens her future classes and emails the managers while her past classes keep her name, a room with classes in it is blocked rather than warned, and status cannot reach 'archived' by hand |

`supabase/seed.sql` runs automatically on `db reset` and seeds Reform Collective as tenant one — **synthetic data only**, every address `@example.com`. One studio, one location, two rooms, four class types, three instructors, owner/manager/front-desk/instructor logins, four plans, a thirteen-class week materialised 26 weeks back and 4 weeks forward, and 30 members across six cohorts with attendance to match. The cohorts exist so the AI features have something real to read: five members drifting into `retention_risk`, four `new_member_stalled`, one `past_due` membership, four who never returned after one class. Attendance is generated from a deterministic hash rather than `random()` - but **it is not actually deterministic, and the claim that every reset produces an identical database is false.** The hash is `hashtextextended(member.id || occurrence.id, 42)`, member ids are pinned literals, and `class_occurrences.id` defaults to `gen_random_uuid()`. Half the hash input is therefore fresh on every reset: two back-to-back resets minutes apart gave 911 and 954 historical bookings, 732 and 791 check-ins. It is the same shape as the `provision_studio()` bug recorded below - a deterministic hash over a non-deterministic id - and the fix is to derive occurrence ids from (series, date) rather than to reseed the hash. Unfixed. No suite depends on it today, because every suite builds its own fixtures; a future one that read seed data would be testing a different database each run. Reform Collective is seeded with a **terracotta** accent (`#B85C38`) on Warm, deliberately not `#BEF738`: it had a null accent, so every member screen fell back to Studiior's lime and the white-label promise was invisible in every screenshot and demo - the one thing the theming work exists to prove. The seed also carries class-type descriptions that say what a class is and what to bring, and instructor bios and certifications, because the member's class detail screen shows them and an empty section there is indistinguishable from a broken query - the same trap the CRM tables sat in for months.

It also seeds the CRM tables — notes (including a `managers_only` one, so that policy has a fixture), goals, tags — and rebuilds the timeline at the end. Those three tables had existed since migration 001 with nothing ever writing to them, which meant every CRM section rendered its empty state in every environment and an empty state was indistinguishable from a broken query.

The vertical slice is built: Next.js App Router + TypeScript + Tailwind, staff app on `localhost:3000` and the member PWA on `{slug}.localhost:3000`. Staff sign in, see the week, create a class; a member signs in on the studio subdomain and books it through `book_class()`; front desk checks them in. See `docs/SLICE.md` for how to run it and which login to use for which role.

Every database call goes through a request-scoped client carrying the user's session, so RLS applies to all of it. There is no service-role client in the codebase and there should never be one — migration 004 exists because the pre-login slug lookup needed a policy, not a key.

Rooms, class types and instructors have list/create/edit screens at `/rooms`, `/class-types`, `/instructors` — Owner and Manager, per Permissions §3 and §4, enforced by the existing `*_manager_write` policies. The dashboard checklist links to all three. An instructor is a teaching record with `staff_id` null: no login, no invite.
